### Managing Hybrid Solid-State Drives Using Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13105v1)
- **Authors**: Qian Wei, Yi Li, Zehao Chen, Zhaoyan Shen, Dongxiao Yu, Bingzhe Li
- **Abstract**: Hybrid Solid-State Drives (SSDs), which integrate several types of flash cells (e.g., single-level cell (SLC) and multiple-level cell (MLC)) in a single drive and enable them to convert between each other, are designed to deliver both high performance and high storage capacity. However, compared to traditional SSDs, hybrid SSDs also introduce a much larger design space, resulting in higher optimization complexity due to more design factors involved, including flash conversion timing and data migration between different flash cells, etc. To address these challenges, large language models (LLMs) could be a promising technique, as they excel in handling complex, high-dimensional parameter space exploration by leveraging their advanced capability to identify patterns and optimize solutions. Recent works have started exploring the use of LLMs to optimize computer systems. However, to the best of our knowledge, no study has focused on optimizing SSDs with the assistance of LLMs. In this work, we explore the potential of LLMs in understanding and efficiently managing hybrid SSD design space. Specifically, two important questions are exploited and analyzed: 1) Can LLMs offer optimization potential for Hybrid SSD management? 2) How to leverage LLMs for the performance and efficiency of hybrid SSD optimization? Based on the observations of exploration, we propose a comprehensive auto-tuning framework for hybrid SSDs, integrating LLMs to recommend customized configurations using calibration prompts derived from hardware, system, and workload information. Experimental results reveal a 62.35% improvement in throughput and a 57.99% decrease in write amplification compared to the default hybrid SSD configurations achieved with the incorporation of LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for managing hybrid Solid-State Drives (SSDs).  Hybrid SSDs integrate different flash cell types (e.g., SLC, MLC) and offer a trade-off between performance and capacity. The paper argues that the larger design space of hybrid SSDs compared to traditional SSDs creates higher optimization complexity.  The authors investigate whether LLMs can effectively manage this complexity.  They propose LLM-hybridSSD, an auto-tuning framework that uses LLMs to recommend customized configurations based on hardware, system, and workload information. Experiments show that LLM-hybridSSD can improve throughput and reduce write amplification compared to default hybrid SSD configurations.  The paper categorizes the hybrid SSD parameter space into SSD-specific, workload-related, and strategy-related parameters for LLM tuning and identifies performance-sensitive parameters for efficiency. It discusses challenges such as developing a comprehensive tuning framework, managing costs, designing the parameter space, and ensuring failure resilience.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of LLMs to optimize SSD management, specifically hybrid SSDs, is a relatively novel idea. While LLMs have been used for system optimization in other areas, this paper explores a new domain. The core idea of using LLMs to handle the complex, multi-dimensional optimization problem posed by hybrid SSDs is promising.

*   **Significance:** Optimizing hybrid SSDs is important because they offer a balance between performance and cost. A more efficient management strategy can lead to better overall system performance, longer SSD lifespan, and reduced power consumption. This paper opens the door for using LLMs to solve complex storage management problems. The identification of performance-sensitive parameters is a valuable contribution. However, the paper does lack a strong comparison with recent RL-based methods by considering more sophisticated RL techniques like DDPG, PPO, or SAC instead of RL-CSSD, a relatively simpler RL approach.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The problem is well-defined and motivated. The paper clearly explains the challenges of managing hybrid SSDs and why LLMs might be suitable.
    *   The LLM-hybridSSD framework is a good starting point. The paper outlines the key components and workflow.
    *   The experimental results show promising improvements in throughput and write amplification.
    *   The categorization of the parameter space and the identification of performance-sensitive parameters are valuable insights.
    *   The paper addresses limitations and proposes future research directions (e.g., integrating the tuner directly into the SSD controller, fine-tuning locally deployed LLMs).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited LLM Exploration:** The reliance primarily on GPT-4 (accessed via API) raises concerns about cost and accessibility for other researchers. While the paper mentions local deployment of Llama and DeepSeek, the performance is not very competitive, limiting the robustness of the experiments.
    *   **Simplicity of LLM usage:** The LLMs are used in a relatively straightforward way (prompt engineering for configuration suggestions). More sophisticated LLM capabilities, such as few-shot learning or fine-tuning for SSD management tasks, could have been explored.
    *   **Limited Comparison to Existing Methods:** The comparison against existing AI-driven SSD management techniques (e.g., RL-CSSD and HAML) is not comprehensive enough. It would be valuable to compare against more recent and advanced RL-based approaches and provide a more detailed analysis of the trade-offs. The analysis of the overhead of different LLMs lacks details. Quantifying the actual monetary cost (e.g., by AWS credits) is more helpful compared to simply stating 'Cost($)'.
    *   **Limited Real-World Validation:** The experimental results are based on a simulator. Testing the framework on real hardware would be more convincing.
    *   **Potential Overfitting:** Optimizing for particular workloads in the simulator could lead to overfitting, meaning the framework might not generalize well to other workloads.
    *   **Scalability Concerns:**  The sliding window approach for preventing token overflow needs more detailed explanation of how it scales to larger and more complex workloads.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper demonstrates the potential of LLMs in managing hybrid SSDs. This could spur further research in this area, leading to more sophisticated LLM-based storage management techniques.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel idea with promising results but has some significant limitations. The core idea of using LLMs for hybrid SSD management is solid, and the LLM-hybridSSD framework is a reasonable starting point. However, the limited exploration of LLM capabilities, the reliance on simulations, the limitations of comparisons to current and advanced state-of-the-art technologies, and scalability concerns prevent a higher score. Future works addressing these limitations could have significant impact. The paper provides valuable insights and sets the stage for further research but needs to be significantly strengthened to demonstrate its full potential.

### ClearSight: Visual Signal Enhancement for Object Hallucination Mitigation in Multimodal Large language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13107v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Yin, Guangzong Si, Zilei Wang
- **Abstract**: Contrastive decoding strategies are widely used to mitigate object hallucinations in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). By reducing over-reliance on language priors, these strategies ensure that generated content remains closely grounded in visual inputs, producing contextually accurate outputs. Since contrastive decoding requires no additional training or external tools, it offers both computational efficiency and versatility, making it highly attractive. However, these methods present two main limitations: (1) bluntly suppressing language priors can compromise coherence and accuracy of generated content, and (2) processing contrastive inputs adds computational load, significantly slowing inference speed. To address these challenges, we propose Visual Amplification Fusion (VAF), a plug-and-play technique that enhances attention to visual signals within the model's middle layers, where modality fusion predominantly occurs. This approach enables more effective capture of visual features, reducing the model's bias toward language modality. Experimental results demonstrate that VAF significantly reduces hallucinations across various MLLMs without affecting inference speed, while maintaining coherence and accuracy in generated outputs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "ClearSight: Visual Signal Enhancement for Object Hallucination Mitigation in Multimodal Large Language Models" addresses the problem of object hallucinations in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). It proposes a novel, training-free technique called Visual Amplification Fusion (VAF) to enhance the model's attention to visual signals during modality fusion.  VAF amplifies visual signals within the MLLM's middle layers, where modality fusion primarily occurs, without negatively impacting inference speed or content quality. The paper presents experimental results on multiple benchmarks, demonstrating that VAF effectively reduces hallucinations across various MLLMs while maintaining coherence and accuracy in generated outputs. They analyze the limitations of Contrastive Decoding strategies and present a strong case for their new approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in the specific approach to address the hallucination problem. While contrastive decoding methods are well-established, VAF's training-free visual enhancement technique targeting the middle layers is a novel solution. The analysis of where modality fusion happens, and identifying the need to emphasize vision inputs at that point is valuable. The insight that the language prior bias is not due to over-weighting the language modality, but under-weighting vision during modality fusion is a key component of the paper's novelty. The method, although simple in implementation, is justified by thorough analysis and shows strong results.

*   **Significance:**  Object hallucination is a major limitation of MLLMs, and the paper tackles this problem directly and effectively. VAF's ability to reduce hallucinations without sacrificing inference speed or content quality is significant. It provides a practical, plug-and-play solution that can be readily applied to existing MLLMs.  The paper's potential impact on the field is considerable, particularly in applications where accurate grounding in visual inputs is critical (e.g., medical diagnosis, autonomous driving). The paper provides a clear argument on the drawbacks of Contrastive Decoding strategies in some cases, which will be useful for future research in this area.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined problem:**  The paper clearly articulates the object hallucination problem in MLLMs.
    *   **Thorough analysis:**  The paper provides a detailed analysis of the limitations of existing contrastive decoding methods, the modality fusion process in MLLMs, and the importance of visual information.
    *   **Effective method:**  The VAF method is shown to be effective in reducing hallucinations without affecting inference speed or content quality.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:**  The paper presents a wide range of experimental results on multiple benchmarks, demonstrating the effectiveness of VAF across various MLLMs.
    *   **Clear writing:**  The paper is well-written and easy to understand.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the method doesn't need *training*, the hyperparameters *α* and *β* need to be tuned. The process of tuning these parameters is not discussed and is crucial for reproducibility and adoption.
    *   The overall improvement from VAF appears to be somewhat limited. For example, the improvement in POPE is about 2-3%. A rigorous statistical test should be done to determine the significance of this improvement.
    *   The paper focuses on middle-layer vision inputs. Could the approach be improved by also adjusting/augmenting the initial vision encoder outputs?
    *   The ablation studies (particularly on suppression coefficient β) could be strengthened with more detailed discussion of the trade-offs.

*   **Potential Influence:** The work has the potential to influence future research on mitigating hallucinations in MLLMs. VAF's plug-and-play nature and its focus on enhancing visual signals during modality fusion can inspire other researchers to develop similar techniques. The paper's analysis of the limitations of contrastive decoding methods can also guide future research in that area.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and effective training-free approach to mitigate object hallucinations in MLLMs. The method is well-motivated by thorough analysis and demonstrated through comprehensive experiments. While the quantitative improvements, although consistent, are relatively small (2-3%), and the tuning process is somewhat opaque, the paper represents a valuable contribution to the field and its potential to improve the accuracy and reliability of MLLMs in real-world applications. Furthermore, the discussion on the limitations of Contrastive Decoding strategies adds to the paper's value.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Lifting the Veil on Visual Information Flow in MLLMs: Unlocking Pathways to Faster Inference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13108v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Yin, Guangzong Si, Zilei Wang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) improve performance on vision-language tasks by integrating visual features from pre-trained vision encoders into large language models (LLMs). However, how MLLMs process and utilize visual information remains unclear. In this paper, a shift in the dominant flow of visual information is uncovered: (1) in shallow layers, strong interactions are observed between image tokens and instruction tokens, where most visual information is injected into instruction tokens to form cross-modal semantic representations; (2) in deeper layers, image tokens primarily interact with each other, aggregating the remaining visual information to optimize semantic representations within visual modality. Based on these insights, we propose Hierarchical Modality-Aware Pruning (HiMAP), a plug-and-play inference acceleration method that dynamically prunes image tokens at specific layers, reducing computational costs by approximately 65% without sacrificing performance. Our findings offer a new understanding of visual information processing in MLLMs and provide a state-of-the-art solution for efficient inference.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Lifting the Veil on Visual Information Flow in MLLMs: Unlocking Pathways to Faster Inference" investigates how Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) process visual information. The authors uncover a shift in the dominant flow of visual information across network layers: in shallow layers, image tokens primarily interact with instruction tokens for cross-modal representation learning, while in deeper layers, image tokens interact more with each other to refine visual representations. Based on this insight, the authors propose Hierarchical Modality-Aware Pruning (HiMAP), a dynamic pruning technique that strategically removes image tokens at specific layers, leading to a significant reduction in computational cost (approximately 65%) without sacrificing performance. HiMAP's effectiveness is validated across diverse vision-language tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Insight:** The core contribution is the novel observation of phased visual information processing in MLLMs, with distinct roles for shallow and deep layers. This insight goes beyond simply applying existing pruning methods and provides a deeper understanding of MLLM internals. This shift in visual token dynamics has not been documented before.
    *   **Practical Application:** The HiMAP method is a direct and valuable consequence of the core insight.  It's a practical approach to reducing inference costs in MLLMs, which is a pressing concern given their resource demands. The code availability enhances reproducibility.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper provides a thorough evaluation across various vision-language benchmarks, ensuring the effectiveness of HiMAP in diverse scenarios. The comparisons against other methods (like FastV) provide further context for the performance gains.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The inclusion of ablation studies strengthens the paper by demonstrating the importance of both the shallow-layer and deeper-layer pruning modules, as well as the importance of the parameters *K* and *R*.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The analysis primarily focuses on one model family (LLaVA).  While the findings are compelling, the generality of the conclusions might be limited. Testing on more diverse architectures (e.g., models with different visual encoders or fusion mechanisms) would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Incremental Innovation on Pruning:** The core novelty lies in the *insight* about visual information flow, not necessarily the pruning *technique* itself. Dynamic pruning has been explored before. HiMAP's innovation lies in *how* it dynamically prunes based on the discovered insights, but the underlying pruning mechanisms are not fundamentally novel.
    *   **Limited Exploration of the "Why":** While the paper observes and utilizes the shift in visual information flow, it offers less in-depth explanation about *why* this phenomenon occurs.  Exploring potential reasons for this phased processing (e.g., what information is being extracted in each phase, and how the model's training objectives encourage this behavior) could add depth.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Impact on MLLM Inference:** HiMAP offers a concrete solution to accelerate inference, which is important for deploying MLLMs in real-world applications.
    *   **Increased Understanding:** The findings contribute to a better understanding of MLLM internals, which can inspire future research on more efficient and effective architectures.
    *   **Potential for Further Research:** The insights about phased visual information processing could guide the design of new training strategies or architectural modifications that further optimize MLLM performance.

*   **Novelty:**

     *   The discovery and explanation of shift in visual token dynamics is the primary novel contribution, rather than the implementation of the pruning method itself.

**Overall Assessment and Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of multimodal learning by uncovering a novel insight into how visual information is processed within MLLMs. HiMAP provides a practical approach to accelerate inference, and the thorough evaluation strengthens the paper's conclusions. While the pruning mechanism itself is not entirely novel, its design is directly and successfully influenced by the novel observation.  The paper's impact is somewhat limited by its focus on a single model family and the relative lack of a deeper dive into the reasons behind the phased processing. Overall, the paper has clear novelty, impact, and solid experimental support.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Code-Driven Inductive Synthesis: Enhancing Reasoning Abilities of Large Language Models with Sequences
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13109v1)
- **Authors**: Kedi Chen, Zhikai Lei, Fan Zhang, Yinqi Zhang, Qin Chen, Jie Zhou, Liang He, Qipeng Guo, Kai Chen, Wei Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large language models make remarkable progress in reasoning capabilities. Existing works focus mainly on deductive reasoning tasks (e.g., code and math), while another type of reasoning mode that better aligns with human learning, inductive reasoning, is not well studied. We attribute the reason to the fact that obtaining high-quality process supervision data is challenging for inductive reasoning. Towards this end, we novelly employ number sequences as the source of inductive reasoning data. We package sequences into algorithmic problems to find the general term of each sequence through a code solution. In this way, we can verify whether the code solution holds for any term in the current sequence, and inject case-based supervision signals by using code unit tests. We build a sequence synthetic data pipeline and form a training dataset CodeSeq. Experimental results show that the models tuned with CodeSeq improve on both code and comprehensive reasoning benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Code-Driven Inductive Synthesis: Enhancing Reasoning Abilities of Large Language Models with Sequences":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the under-explored area of inductive reasoning in large language models (LLMs). Recognizing the challenge of obtaining high-quality process supervision data for inductive reasoning, the authors propose a novel approach: using number sequences as the source of data.  They package these sequences into algorithmic problems, where the LLM is tasked with finding the general term of the sequence represented as a code solution.  The code solutions can be verified, and case-based supervision injected via unit tests. The authors create a sequence synthetic data pipeline and the resulting dataset, CodeSeq. Experiments demonstrate that LLMs fine-tuned on CodeSeq exhibit improved performance on code generation and comprehensive reasoning benchmarks, also improving the models inductive reasoning capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the specific application of number sequences *combined with* the code generation paradigm *specifically to address the issue of inductive reasoning*. Utilizing sequences themselves isn't entirely new, and code generation for reasoning is also explored elsewhere, but the particular combination and its rationale are novel. The method of injecting supervision signals through code unit tests is a valuable contribution. The construction of the CodeSeq dataset itself is a significant undertaking, requiring a pipeline of filtering, problem generation, and supervision injection.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important gap: inductive reasoning in LLMs. While deductive reasoning receives significant attention, inductive reasoning is arguably more fundamental to human-like learning and generalization. By demonstrating a method to improve inductive reasoning through code-driven synthesis and supervision, the paper opens up a new avenue of research. The observed improvements on code generation benchmarks are also a valuable contribution, suggesting that enhancing inductive reasoning can lead to benefits in other areas. However, the scale of improvements, although present, might not be *transformative*.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper identifies a clear and important research gap.
    *   **Novel Approach:**  The use of number sequences and code unit tests is a clever way to address the challenges of inductive reasoning supervision.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The synthetic data generation pipeline is well-described, and the experiments are thorough, including ablation studies.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides empirical evidence supporting the effectiveness of CodeSeq.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The focus on number sequences, while a good starting point, might limit the generalizability of the approach to other forms of inductive reasoning.  The paper would be strengthened by discussing potential extensions to other data types and reasoning tasks.
    *   **Incremental Improvements:** While improvements are observed, they are not overwhelmingly large. This raises questions about the practical impact and the limitations of the current approach. The Claude-3.5-Sonnet comparison in Figure 3 is interesting, but it highlights the gap that still exists.
    *   **Dataset Dependence:**  The improvements are inherently tied to the quality and diversity of the CodeSeq dataset. The paper acknowledges the limitations of using only sequences and proposes future research directions.
    *   **Comparison:** While GPT-4o and Claude-3.5-Sonnet are mentioned, the paper primarily uses fine-tuning on Qwen2.5-7B and LLAMA3-8B. It is not clear if improvements on CodeSeq could get closer to GPT-4o's performance on the mentioned tasks.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in several areas:
    *   **Inductive Reasoning:** It provides a concrete method for improving inductive reasoning in LLMs.
    *   **Synthetic Data Generation:** The data generation pipeline can serve as a template for other reasoning tasks.
    *   **Code-Driven Reasoning:** It reinforces the idea that code generation can be a powerful tool for reasoning in LLMs.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to address a significant challenge in the field of LLMs. The method of injecting supervision signals is a key strength. However, the limited scope of using number sequences and the incremental nature of the improvements prevent it from being a truly transformative work. The paper lays a strong foundation and opens up interesting avenues for future research. For these reasons, a score of 7 is appropriate.

**Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### DTGBrepGen: A Novel B-rep Generative Model through Decoupling Topology and Geometry
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13110v1)
- **Authors**: Jing Li, Yihang Fu, Falai Chen
- **Abstract**: Boundary representation (B-rep) of geometric models is a fundamental format in Computer-Aided Design (CAD). However, automatically generating valid and high-quality B-rep models remains challenging due to the complex interdependence between the topology and geometry of the models. Existing methods tend to prioritize geometric representation while giving insufficient attention to topological constraints, making it difficult to maintain structural validity and geometric accuracy. In this paper, we propose DTGBrepGen, a novel topology-geometry decoupled framework for B-rep generation that explicitly addresses both aspects. Our approach first generates valid topological structures through a two-stage process that independently models edge-face and edge-vertex adjacency relationships. Subsequently, we employ Transformer-based diffusion models for sequential geometry generation, progressively generating vertex coordinates, followed by edge geometries and face geometries which are represented as B-splines. Extensive experiments on diverse CAD datasets show that DTGBrepGen significantly outperforms existing methods in both topological validity and geometric accuracy, achieving higher validity rates and producing more diverse and realistic B-reps. Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/jinli99/DTGBrepGen.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DTGBrepGen, a novel framework for generating Boundary Representation (B-rep) models, a fundamental format in Computer-Aided Design (CAD).  The core innovation is decoupling the generation of topological structure from geometric attributes. It first generates a valid topological structure through a two-stage process independently modeling edge-face and edge-vertex adjacencies using Transformer encoder-decoder networks. Then, Transformer-based diffusion models are used to sequentially generate geometric attributes like vertex coordinates, edge geometries (B-splines), and face geometries (B-splines).  Experiments on diverse CAD datasets demonstrate significant improvements in both topological validity and geometric accuracy compared to existing methods. The code is publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the *explicit decoupling* of topology and geometry generation in B-rep modeling. Existing B-rep generation methods often entangle these two aspects, leading to difficulties in maintaining structural validity. The two-stage Transformer approach to *independently modeling edge-face and edge-vertex adjacencies* for topology generation is also a significant contribution. Further, the use of B-spline representations throughout the *sequential geometry generation pipeline* provides superior accuracy and compactness compared to point-based methods. This is a solid contribution that moves beyond generating simple shapes or relying heavily on post-processing to correct topology.
* **Significance:** High-quality B-rep generation is a crucial problem in CAD.  The ability to automatically generate diverse and valid B-reps could have a significant impact on design exploration, rapid prototyping, and manufacturing. DTGBrepGen improves the state-of-the-art by increasing the generation of valid and diverse B-rep models. It alleviates prior issues of failing to maintain structural validity. It provides more accurate geometries compared to other baselines. The adoption of B-spline allows the generation of B-rep models with mathematically precise geometric representations compared to point sampling methods.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Decoupled Approach:** Addressing the core challenge of intertwined topology and geometry.
    * **Two-stage Topology Generation:**  Offers a robust way to generate valid topological structures by tackling edge-face and edge-vertex constraints systematically.
    * **B-spline Representation:** Utilizing B-splines allows for accurate and compact geometric representations, enabling direct learning of control point distributions.
    * **Transformer and Diffusion Models:** State-of-the-art generative models leverage the advantages of Transformers and diffusion models,
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Thorough experiments on several datasets comparing against strong baselines with both distribution and CAD metrics.
    * **Public Code:** Facilitates reproducibility and further research.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** The framework is complex, involving multiple Transformer models and diffusion processes, which might be difficult to adapt or extend.
    * **Limitations of Topology Network:** Results indicate that there is room for improvement for the topology generation networks. Although it achieves high accuracy and uniqueness, it does not always lead to high-quality or successful geometry generation due to a mismatch in learned data distribution.
    * **Limited Datasets:** While experiments are conducted on several datasets, they are still limited compared to the vast design space of CAD models. The limitations discussed in the paper on generating more complex geometric features indicates that there is still work that needs to be done.
* **Potential Influence:** DTGBrepGen provides a more principled approach to B-rep generation. The idea of decoupling topology and geometry can influence future research in CAD model generation. Further research that builds on this approach can explore more complex CAD models. The adoption of B-spline geometries can allow the generation of precise, mathematically accurate B-rep models.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7.  The decoupling of topology and geometry, along with the novel two-stage topology generation, and the leveraging of B-splines for geometric representations are significant contributions that address a core challenge in B-rep generation. The experimental results support the claim of improved performance.  The approach offers a principled method for generating B-reps. However, the complexity of the method, limitations with existing models and datasets prevents the paper from being a high-impact paper. The paper's significance would be higher if the complexity of the framework was more manageable for future extensions to complex shapes.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MM-Spatial: Exploring 3D Spatial Understanding in Multimodal LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13111v1)
- **Authors**: Erik Daxberger, Nina Wenzel, David Griffiths, Haiming Gang, Justin Lazarow, Gefen Kohavi, Kai Kang, Marcin Eichner, Yinfei Yang, Afshin Dehghan, Peter Grasch
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) excel at 2D visual understanding but remain limited in their ability to reason about 3D space. In this work, we leverage large-scale high-quality 3D scene data with open-set annotations to introduce 1) a novel supervised fine-tuning dataset and 2) a new evaluation benchmark, focused on indoor scenes. Our Cubify Anything VQA (CA-VQA) data covers diverse spatial tasks including spatial relationship prediction, metric size and distance estimation, and 3D grounding. We show that CA-VQA enables us to train MM-Spatial, a strong generalist MLLM that also achieves state-of-the-art performance on 3D spatial understanding benchmarks, including our own. We show how incorporating metric depth and multi-view inputs (provided in CA-VQA) can further improve 3D understanding, and demonstrate that data alone allows our model to achieve depth perception capabilities comparable to dedicated monocular depth estimation models. We will publish our SFT dataset and benchmark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the MM-Spatial paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MM-Spatial: Exploring 3D Spatial Understanding in Multimodal LLMS" introduces a novel approach to improve 3D spatial reasoning capabilities in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The authors address the limitations of existing MLLMs in understanding and reasoning about 3D space, specifically within indoor scenes.  They contribute a new supervised fine-tuning dataset and evaluation benchmark called Cubify Anything VQA (CA-VQA) based on high-quality 3D scene data with open-set annotations. CA-VQA covers diverse spatial tasks such as spatial relationship prediction, metric size/distance estimation, and 3D grounding.  The authors train MM-Spatial, a generalist MLLM, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance on 3D spatial understanding benchmarks, including their own. They explore the impact of incorporating metric depth and multi-view inputs, showing performance improvements. The paper also showcases the possibility of achieving monocular depth estimation capabilities comparable to specialized models through data alone.  The authors make their dataset and benchmark publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents significant novelties:
    * **Dataset (CA-VQA):** The creation of a comprehensive, high-quality dataset focused on 3D spatial reasoning with various input signals (multi-view, depth maps) and diverse task categories is a strong contribution. Its open-set nature is particularly beneficial.  Prior datasets were often limited in scope, annotation quality, or availability.
    * **Benchmark:**  The derived benchmark addresses limitations of existing benchmarks by providing diverse tasks, rich input signals, and reduced susceptibility to language priors, making it a more challenging and vision-reliant evaluation tool. The blind filtering is also a valuable improvement.
    * **Model (MM-Spatial):**  Training a generalist MLLM that achieves state-of-the-art results on multiple 3D spatial understanding benchmarks is noteworthy. The exploration of different input modalities (depth, multi-view) and their impact on performance is also a valuable contribution.
    * **Monocular Depth Estimation:**  The result that an MLLM trained solely on data can achieve near-SOTA monocular depth accuracy is surprising and valuable.

* **Significance:**
    * **Addressing a Key Limitation:** The paper directly tackles the recognized limitation of MLLMs in 3D spatial understanding, a crucial area for applications in robotics, AR/VR, and general visual comprehension.
    * **Improved Performance:**  The empirical results demonstrate significant performance improvements compared to existing MLLMs on spatial reasoning tasks.
    * **Data-Driven Learning:** It highlights the power of high-quality data in enabling MLLMs to learn complex tasks like depth estimation, potentially reducing the need for complex architectures or specialized pre-training strategies.
    * **Community Resource:**  The public release of the dataset and benchmark will facilitate further research in this area, fostering progress in 3D spatial reasoning for MLLMs.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes extensive experiments comparing MM-Spatial to state-of-the-art models across various benchmarks. The ablation studies provide insights into the contributions of different components and input modalities.
    * **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is well-organized and clearly explains the methodology, experiments, and results.
    * **Practical Contributions:** The paper provides both a valuable dataset/benchmark and a strong baseline model, benefiting the research community.
    * **Thorough Analysis**: The blind vs. vision evaluation to evaluate language biases and the discussions around AABB and OBB are insightful.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Indoor Focus:** The dataset and benchmark are primarily focused on indoor scenes. While this allows for high-quality annotations and controlled experiments, it limits the generalization of the findings to outdoor or more complex environments.  This is acknowledged, and extending the scope to outdoor environments is proposed as future work.
    * **Architecture limitations**. While the authors demonstrate a novel data curation methodology, the experiments do not try advanced architectures. They stick to DFN-CLIP/MM1.5. It would be even more significant if the data strategy unlocked more complex architectures.

* **Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of MLLMs and 3D scene understanding by providing a valuable dataset, benchmark, and baseline model. It encourages further research on improving spatial reasoning capabilities in MLLMs and exploring the interplay between data, architectures, and input modalities.
Score: 8

**Justification for the Score:**

A score of 8 is assigned because the paper offers a combination of significant novelty and practical value. The creation of the CA-VQA dataset and benchmark addresses a key limitation in the MLLM field, and the empirical results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach. While the indoor focus and the fact that it is data-driven without innovation in the architecture somewhat limit its broader impact, the paper's contributions are substantial and will likely stimulate further research and development in this area. The public release of the dataset and benchmark further amplifies its value to the community.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### VeriLeaky: Navigating IP Protection vs Utility in Fine-Tuning for LLM-Driven Verilog Coding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13116v1)
- **Authors**: Zeng Wang, Minghao Shao, Mohammed Nabeel, Prithwish Basu Roy, Likhitha Mankali, Jitendra Bhandari, Ramesh Karri, Ozgur Sinanoglu, Muhammad Shafique, Johann Knechtel
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) offer significant potential for coding, yet fine-tuning (FT) with curated data is essential for niche languages like Verilog. Using proprietary intellectual property (IP) for FT presents a serious risk, as FT data can be leaked through LLM inference. This leads to a critical dilemma for design houses: seeking to build externally accessible LLMs offering competitive Verilog coding, how can they leverage in-house IP to enhance FT utility while ensuring IP protection? For the first time in the literature, we study this dilemma. Using LLaMA 3.1-8B, we conduct in-house FT on a baseline Verilog dataset (RTLCoder) supplemented with our own in-house IP, which is validated through multiple tape-outs. To rigorously assess IP leakage, we quantify structural similarity (AST/Dolos) and functional equivalence (Synopsys Formality) between generated codes and our in-house IP. We show that our IP can indeed be leaked, confirming the threat. As defense, we evaluate logic locking of Verilog codes (ASSURE). This offers some level of protection, yet reduces the IP's utility for FT and degrades the LLM's performance. Our study shows the need for novel strategies that are both effective and minimally disruptive to FT, an essential effort for enabling design houses to fully utilize their proprietary IP toward LLM-driven Verilog coding.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "VeriLeaky: Navigating IP Protection vs Utility in Fine-Tuning for LLM-Driven Verilog Coding" investigates the critical dilemma of how to leverage in-house intellectual property (IP) to enhance the fine-tuning (FT) utility of Large Language Models (LLMs) for Verilog coding while simultaneously ensuring IP protection. The authors demonstrate that proprietary IP can be leaked through LLM inference by fine-tuning with LLaMA 3.1-8B. They assess IP leakage using structural similarity (AST/Dolos) and functional equivalence (Synopsys Formality). The paper also explores logic locking as a countermeasure but reveals that it reduces utility and degrades LLM performance. The study highlights the need for novel, effective, and minimally disruptive IP protection strategies to utilize proprietary IP fully towards LLM-driven Verilog coding.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a previously unexplored area of IP leakage in LLM-driven hardware design. The problem is critical given the increasing adoption of LLMs for code generation and the valuable in-house IP that many companies possess.
*   **Relevance:** The research is highly relevant to design houses seeking to build externally accessible LLMs for Verilog coding. The identification and analysis of IP leakage threats are crucial for these organizations.
*   **Thorough Methodology:** The paper employs rigorous assessment techniques, including structural similarity (AST/Dolos) and functional equivalence (Synopsys Formality). It systematically varies FT and inference parameters to evaluate IP leakage.
*   **Practical Experiments:** The experiments are based on a realistic scenario, using in-house IP validated through multiple tape-outs. This enhances the practical implications of the findings.
*   **Countermeasure Evaluation:** The evaluation of logic locking provides insights into its effectiveness and limitations. This helps to guide future research on IP protection strategies.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **LLM Scope:** The study focuses on LLaMA 3.1-8B. While a representative LLM, the field is evolving rapidly. Testing across a wider range of models (different architectures, sizes) would strengthen the generalizability of the results.
*   **Logic Locking Limitations:** The paper relies on a specific logic locking technique (ASSURE). The effectiveness of other locking techniques or more advanced obfuscation strategies could be further investigated. The overhead and area impact of ASSURE is not explicitly quantified.
*   **Prompting Limitation:** The prompts for IP leakage assessment assume direct access to the instructions used during FT. This conservative assumption might not always hold in real-world scenarios.
*   **Limited mitigation approach:** While the paper discusses Logic Locking as a defense, it provides a more detailed analysis of its downsides than its efficacy. The paper would benefit from investigating more sophisticated mitigation techniques beyond logic locking.
*   **Lack of Comparative Analysis:** There is a lack of comparative analysis against the baseline methodology. For instance, providing a direct comparison of the performance of the custom model with and without logic locking would enhance the understanding of its effect on the system's performance.

**Significance and Impact:**

The paper's findings are significant for design houses aiming to leverage LLMs for Verilog coding without compromising their IP. The study raises awareness of IP leakage threats and provides insights into the challenges of IP protection. The results can inform the development of more effective IP protection strategies and guide the responsible use of LLMs in hardware design.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper addresses a novel and relevant problem with a rigorous methodology. It identifies a real threat and provides valuable insights into its mitigation. However, some weaknesses exist, particularly regarding the limited scope of LLMs and logic locking techniques. Therefore, the paper represents a significant contribution to the field but is not an exceptional or groundbreaking work.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Patient-specific radiomic feature selection with reconstructed healthy persona of knee MR images
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13131v1)
- **Authors**: Yaxi Chen, Simin Ni, Aleksandra Ivanova, Shaheer U. Saeed, Rikin Hargunani, Jie Huang, Chaozong Liu, Yipeng Hu
- **Abstract**: Classical radiomic features have been designed to describe image appearance and intensity patterns. These features are directly interpretable and readily understood by radiologists. Compared with end-to-end deep learning (DL) models, lower dimensional parametric models that use such radiomic features offer enhanced interpretability but lower comparative performance in clinical tasks. In this study, we propose an approach where a standard logistic regression model performance is substantially improved by learning to select radiomic features for individual patients, from a pool of candidate features. This approach has potentials to maintain the interpretability of such approaches while offering comparable performance to DL. We also propose to expand the feature pool by generating a patient-specific healthy persona via mask-inpainting using a denoising diffusion model trained on healthy subjects. Such a pathology-free baseline feature set allows further opportunity in novel feature discovery and improved condition classification. We demonstrate our method on multiple clinical tasks of classifying general abnormalities, anterior cruciate ligament tears, and meniscus tears. Experimental results demonstrate that our approach achieved comparable or even superior performance than state-of-the-art DL approaches while offering added interpretability by using radiomic features extracted from images and supplemented by generating healthy personas. Example clinical cases are discussed in-depth to demonstrate the intepretability-enabled utilities such as human-explainable feature discovery and patient-specific location/view selection. These findings highlight the potentials of the combination of subject-specific feature selection with generative models in augmenting radiomic analysis for more interpretable decision-making. The codes are available at: https://github.com/YaxiiC/RadiomicsPersona.git
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary**

The paper proposes a novel framework for knee MRI analysis that combines generative modeling and logistic regression classification. The core idea is to enhance radiomic feature selection by leveraging a "healthy persona" – a patient-specific, pathology-free reconstruction of the knee anatomy generated using a denoising diffusion model (DDPM) trained on healthy subjects.  By comparing radiomic features extracted from the original pathological MRI and the reconstructed healthy persona, the approach aims to improve disease characterization and enable more interpretable analysis. The framework learns to select the most relevant radiomic features for each patient and downstream classification is then performed using a logistic regression classifier with selected features. The method is evaluated on tasks like classifying general abnormalities, ACL tears, and meniscus tears using the MRNet dataset.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:*

The primary novelty lies in the combination of a few elements:

1.  **Patient-Specific Healthy Persona:** The application of a DDPM to generate a pathology-free healthy persona for each patient is a significant contribution. It creates a personalized baseline for comparison, allowing for subtle variations to be identified. Prior work has used generative models for image synthesis and anomaly detection, but this specific application to create a personalized healthy baseline is relatively novel.

2.  **Radiomic Feature Selection:** Learning to select radiomic features on a patient-by-patient basis has merit, although not entirely new. The improvement comes from incorporating features from the healthy persona. Many previous efforts are directed to developing new deep learning features without explicit radiomic interpretation, so selecting radiomic features (extracted from both pathological image and healthy persona) improves explainability, which is important in the medical domain.

3.  **Radiomics and Generative Model Integration:**  While radiomics is a relatively mature field, combining it with DDPMs for generating patient-specific baselines and then using this for feature selection is a distinct contribution. This integration provides a richer feature space for analysis.

*Significance:*

The significance of the work rests on several factors:

1.  **Improved Performance:**  The experimental results show comparable or superior performance to state-of-the-art end-to-end DL approaches on the chosen tasks (abnormality, ACL tear, meniscus tear detection). This demonstrates the practical effectiveness of the method.

2.  **Enhanced Interpretability:**  The use of radiomic features provides a level of interpretability that is often lacking in "black box" deep learning models. The paper discusses how the selected features correlate with known anatomical and pathological characteristics. This is a major advantage in clinical settings, where understanding *why* a model made a certain prediction is crucial. The discussion of specific radiomic features (e.g., entropy, Interquartile Range) in the context of ACL and meniscus tears strengthens the claim of interpretability.

3.  **Potential for Biomarker Discovery:** By identifying key radiomic features, the approach offers the potential to uncover novel imaging biomarkers for knee joint disorders. The patient-specific feature selection process allows for capturing subtle variations that might be missed by traditional methods.

4. **Code Availability:** The open sharing of the code is beneficial for the community to reproduce and build upon this work.

*Weaknesses:*

1.  **Limited Dataset:**  While the MRNet dataset is commonly used, a larger and more diverse dataset would provide stronger evidence for the generalizability of the approach.

2. **Dependence on Pre-defined Radiomic Features:** This is also a strength but also a potential limitation, the feature pool is limited to pre-existing radiomic features, so the framework is incapable of learning novel (not existing radiomic) features. While providing increased interpretability, this restricts the model from learning novel features.

3.  **Comparison Scope:** The paper compares with existing end-to-end methods as a benchmark. The comparison would be stronger if the radiomic feature selection with a standard persona/population-based feature extraction can be established as a comparative method.

4.  **Parameter Sensitivity:** The method requires tuning parameters, such as the feature selection threshold (T). The analysis of parameter sensitivity is useful, but optimizing these parameters can be time-consuming and application-specific.

*Potential Influence:*

The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

*   Encouraging further research into the integration of generative models and radiomics for medical image analysis.
*   Promoting the development of more interpretable AI models for clinical applications.
*   Providing a framework for personalized medicine based on imaging biomarkers.

*Score:*

**Score: 7**

*Rationale:*

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of medical image analysis by combining generative models with radiomics to enhance feature selection and improve interpretability. The demonstrated performance improvements over existing methods, along with the potential for biomarker discovery and code availability, support this evaluation. The patient-specific healthy persona aspect is a key innovation. However, the relatively small dataset, reliance on predefined radiomic features, scope of comparison and parameter sensitivity limit the overall impact to an extent. Nevertheless, the work represents a valuable step towards more explainable and personalized AI in medical imaging.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Are LLMs (Really) Ideological? An IRT-based Analysis and Alignment Tool for Perceived Socio-Economic Bias in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13149v1)
- **Authors**: Jasmin Wachter, Michael Radloff, Maja Smolej, Katharina Kinder-Kurlanda
- **Abstract**: We introduce an Item Response Theory (IRT)-based framework to detect and quantify socioeconomic bias in large language models (LLMs) without relying on subjective human judgments. Unlike traditional methods, IRT accounts for item difficulty, improving ideological bias estimation. We fine-tune two LLM families (Meta-LLaMa 3.2-1B-Instruct and Chat- GPT 3.5) to represent distinct ideological positions and introduce a two-stage approach: (1) modeling response avoidance and (2) estimating perceived bias in answered responses. Our results show that off-the-shelf LLMs often avoid ideological engagement rather than exhibit bias, challenging prior claims of partisanship. This empirically validated framework enhances AI alignment research and promotes fairer AI governance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper introduces an Item Response Theory (IRT)-based framework for detecting and quantifying socio-economic bias in large language models (LLMs).  It moves beyond subjective human judgements by accounting for item difficulty, thereby improving the estimation of ideological bias. The authors fine-tune two LLM families (Meta-LLaMa 3.2-1B-Instruct and Chat-GPT 3.5) to represent distinct ideological positions and propose a two-stage approach: 1) modeling response avoidance and 2) estimating perceived bias in the answered responses.  The results indicate that off-the-shelf LLMs tend to avoid ideological engagement rather than exhibit strong bias, challenging previous claims about partisanship. The authors argue that this framework enhances AI alignment research and promotes fairer AI governance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Strength:** The paper's core novelty lies in its application of IRT to the problem of ideological bias detection in LLMs. Traditional bias detection methods often rely on human judgments or simple accuracy metrics, which can be subjective and fail to account for the nuances of item difficulty. This adaptation of IRT provides a statistically more rigorous approach. The two-stage model addressing response avoidance is also a notable contribution, as it highlights a crucial distinction often overlooked in prior studies.
*   **Weakness:** While the IRT framework itself is not novel, its specific application to this problem domain is. However, the specific details of how the questions in the itemset were curated beyond being based on existing literature on US Ideology requires more detail.

**Significance:**

*   **Strength:** The paper addresses a highly relevant and timely issue: the potential for bias in LLMs. Given the increasing deployment of LLMs in various societal domains, understanding and mitigating bias is crucial for ensuring fairness and trustworthiness. The paper's findings, particularly the observation that LLMs may be avoiding engagement rather than exhibiting strong bias, offer valuable insights for the AI alignment community.
*   **Weakness:** The paper's scope is somewhat limited by its focus on socio-economic bias and its specific application to US political ideology. The generalizability of the framework to other types of bias and different cultural contexts remains to be explored. The empirical evaluation, while solid, is based on two specific LLM families. Expanding the evaluation to a broader range of models would strengthen the paper's conclusions. The "Ideology" scores derived from the experiments are comparative but do not necessarily provide an absolute measure of bias; their meaning and implications require careful consideration.

**Impact:**

*   **Potential:** The paper's framework could serve as a foundation for developing more robust and statistically grounded bias detection tools for LLMs. It also provides a valuable methodology for researchers interested in studying the impact of alignment techniques on ideological bias. The open-text approach and use of a smaller, more controlled training corpus, as opposed to large ideological datasets, for finetuning is a plus and contributes to ethical concerns.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a statistically sound and original approach to measuring ideology in LLMs that directly contrasts prior human-driven or ad-hoc metrics. However, the practical application of this technique, whether the IRT methodology can be applied more broadly, and the limits of the dataset used in this paper do limit the paper's impact.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Triad: Empowering LMM-based Anomaly Detection with Vision Expert-guided Visual Tokenizer and Manufacturing Process
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13184v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanze Li, Shihao Yuan, Haolin Wang, Qizhang Li, Ming Liu, Chen Xu, Guangming Shi, Wangmeng Zuo
- **Abstract**: Although recent methods have tried to introduce large multimodal models (LMMs) into industrial anomaly detection (IAD), their generalization in the IAD field is far inferior to that for general purposes. We summarize the main reasons for this gap into two aspects. On one hand, general-purpose LMMs lack cognition of defects in the visual modality, thereby failing to sufficiently focus on defect areas. Therefore, we propose to modify the AnyRes structure of the LLaVA model, providing the potential anomalous areas identified by existing IAD models to the LMMs. On the other hand, existing methods mainly focus on identifying defects by learning defect patterns or comparing with normal samples, yet they fall short of understanding the causes of these defects. Considering that the generation of defects is closely related to the manufacturing process, we propose a manufacturing-driven IAD paradigm. An instruction-tuning dataset for IAD (InstructIAD) and a data organization approach for Chain-of-Thought with manufacturing (CoT-M) are designed to leverage the manufacturing process for IAD. Based on the above two modifications, we present Triad, a novel LMM-based method incorporating an expert-guided region-of-interest tokenizer and manufacturing process for industrial anomaly detection. Extensive experiments show that our Triad not only demonstrates competitive performance against current LMMs but also achieves further improved accuracy when equipped with manufacturing processes. Source code, training data, and pre-trained models will be publicly available at https://github.com/tzjtatata/Triad.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Triad: Empowering LMM-based Anomaly Detection with Vision Expert-guided Visual Tokenizer and Manufacturing Process":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitations of applying general-purpose Large Multimodal Models (LMMs) to industrial anomaly detection (IAD). The authors identify two key issues: LMMs lack specific visual cognition for defects and fail to understand the relationship between defects and manufacturing processes. To overcome these limitations, the paper introduces Triad, an LMM-based method incorporating an expert-guided region-of-interest (EG-RoI) tokenizer and a manufacturing-driven IAD paradigm. EG-RoI focuses the LMM on potential defect areas identified by existing IAD models. The manufacturing-driven paradigm uses an instruction-tuning dataset (InstructIAD) and a Chain-of-Thought approach with manufacturing details (CoT-M) to leverage process knowledge. Experiments demonstrate improved performance against other LMMs, especially when the manufacturing process is considered. The authors provide datasets and code for public access.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel components. The EG-RoI tokenizer, which uses existing IAD models to guide LMM's attention towards defect regions, is a worthwhile contribution. The combination of this with the manufacturing process reasoning is also an advance. The creation of the InstructIAD dataset and CoT-M is significant, explicitly aiming to inject manufacturing knowledge into the LMM. While utilizing manufacturing process knowledge is conceptually intuitive, its integration into an LMM for anomaly detection is a valuable advance.

**Significance:** The paper has several strengths contributing to its significance. First, the identification of limitations in using general-purpose LMMs for IAD is well-articulated and provides a clear motivation for the research. Secondly, the empirical results are convincing. The paper demonstrates clear improvements over existing methods, highlighting the benefits of both EG-RoI and manufacturing process integration. Publicly releasing the dataset and code will facilitate further research. The improved accuracy of anomaly detection in industrial contexts is a practical contribution. However, the study primarily tests on benchmark datasets like MVTec-AD and WFDD, which are not necessarily representative of the complexities of real-world manufacturing environments.

**Weaknesses:** The approach's dependence on existing IAD models for RoI detection is a potential bottleneck. The performance of Triad is inherently limited by the accuracy of these initial models.  The reliance on GPT for generating and augmenting the data raises concerns about potential biases and inaccuracies in the manufacturing process information. Although the authors state a manual filtering step to remove hallucinations and errors, the potential impact on the learned relationships remains a concern. While the use of Chain-of-Thought reasoning is motivated, its implementation seems relatively straightforward, primarily consisting of concatenating information about the manufacturing process.  More sophisticated reasoning techniques could potentially be explored.

**Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in the application of LMMs to industrial anomaly detection. It provides a practical framework for integrating domain-specific knowledge into these models. The InstructIAD dataset and CoT-M strategy can serve as a valuable resource for researchers. The modular design of Triad allows for the substitution of different vision experts or the incorporation of more advanced reasoning techniques, thus furthering future innovation.

**Justification for Score:** While the paper presents a valuable contribution to the field, some limitations prevent it from achieving a higher score. The reliance on existing IAD models, the potential for bias in the generated data, and the relatively simple reasoning strategy are all factors. However, the novelty of integrating these components, the demonstrated performance improvements, and the potential for future research warrant a favorable evaluation.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### 3DAxisPrompt: Promoting the 3D Grounding and Reasoning in GPT-4o
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13185v1)
- **Authors**: Dingning Liu, Cheng Wang, Peng Gao, Renrui Zhang, Xinzhu Ma, Yuan Meng, Zhihui Wang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) exhibit impressive capabilities across a variety of tasks, especially when equipped with carefully designed visual prompts. However, existing studies primarily focus on logical reasoning and visual understanding, while the capability of MLLMs to operate effectively in 3D vision remains an ongoing area of exploration. In this paper, we introduce a novel visual prompting method, called 3DAxisPrompt, to elicit the 3D understanding capabilities of MLLMs in real-world scenes. More specifically, our method leverages the 3D coordinate axis and masks generated from the Segment Anything Model (SAM) to provide explicit geometric priors to MLLMs and then extend their impressive 2D grounding and reasoning ability to real-world 3D scenarios. Besides, we first provide a thorough investigation of the potential visual prompting formats and conclude our findings to reveal the potential and limits of 3D understanding capabilities in GPT-4o, as a representative of MLLMs. Finally, we build evaluation environments with four datasets, i.e., ScanRefer, ScanNet, FMB, and nuScene datasets, covering various 3D tasks. Based on this, we conduct extensive quantitative and qualitative experiments, which demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method. Overall, our study reveals that MLLMs, with the help of 3DAxisPrompt, can effectively perceive an object's 3D position in real-world scenarios. Nevertheless, a single prompt engineering approach does not consistently achieve the best outcomes for all 3D tasks. This study highlights the feasibility of leveraging MLLMs for 3D vision grounding/reasoning with prompt engineering techniques.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "3DAxisPrompt: Promoting the 3D Grounding and Reasoning in GPT-40".

**Summary:**

The paper introduces 3DAxisPrompt, a novel visual prompting method designed to enhance the 3D understanding capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs), specifically focusing on GPT-40. The approach involves embedding a 3D coordinate axis and object masks (generated via SAM – Segment Anything Model) into rendered images of 3D point clouds. By providing explicit geometric and semantic priors, the authors aim to extend MLLMs' 2D grounding/reasoning abilities to real-world 3D scenarios.  The authors conduct a thorough investigation of different visual prompt formats to identify the most effective cues for 3D understanding and evaluate the performance of 3DAxisPrompt across various 3D tasks using datasets like ScanRefer, ScanNet, FMB, and nuScene. The paper demonstrates that MLLMs, when augmented with 3DAxisPrompt, can effectively perceive an object's 3D position and perform tasks such as 3D localization, route planning, and robot action prediction.  They also explore the effect of Chain of Thought prompting combined with 3DAxisPrompt. The authors additionally test other LLMs with 3DAxisPrompt and the combination of COT and 3DAxisPrompt, including Deepseek-R1, Claude 3.7 Sonnet, and Grok-3.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper exhibits a reasonable degree of novelty in its approach. While visual prompting for MLLMs is an established area, specifically the application of prompting for 3D understanding, is relatively new. The idea of using a 3D coordinate axis alongside SAM-generated masks to provide geometric and semantic context is a reasonably novel combination.

**Significance and Impact:**

*   **Potential Impact:** The potential impact of the work lies in broadening the applicability of MLLMs to 3D vision tasks. Enabling MLLMs to understand and reason about 3D scenes could open doors for various applications in robotics, autonomous navigation, augmented reality, and virtual environments.
*   **Limited Scope:** The paper primarily focuses on improving the 3D grounding and reasoning capabilities of GPT-40 in simulated or reconstructed 3D environments.  It doesn't address the challenges of applying these techniques to real-time 3D perception from sensor data in the physical world, which is a critical aspect for many real-world applications. The tasks are relatively simple and don't explore deep 3D reasoning.
*   **Reliance on SAM:** The reliance on SAM for generating object masks introduces a dependency on the performance of SAM. Any errors or limitations in SAM's segmentation capabilities will directly impact the effectiveness of 3DAxisPrompt.
*   **Generality to other LLMs:** The results show, that Claude 3.7 Sonnet and Deepseek-R1 also have improved localization capabilities in conjunction with 3DAxisPrompt, thus improving the generality to other LLMs besides GPT-4o.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Investigation:** The paper presents a thorough investigation of different visual prompting formats, providing valuable insights into the types of cues that are most effective for 3D understanding.
*   **Quantitative and Qualitative Results:** The paper includes both quantitative and qualitative experiments, providing a well-rounded evaluation of the proposed method.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and clearly presents the methodology, experiments, and results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Real-World Applicability:** As mentioned above, the focus on simulated environments limits the immediate applicability of the techniques to real-world scenarios.
*   **Over-reliance on GPT-40:** While testing other LLMs, their testing and analysis are limited and much of the analysis and investigation focuses on GPT-4o specifically.
*   **Ablation studies:** While ablation studies are performed, they focus primarily on the number of images and the presence/absence of axis labels and ticks. This is a limited scope and could be expanded upon.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a novel and interesting approach for improving the 3D understanding capabilities of MLLMs. The use of 3DAxisPrompt offers a promising way to inject geometric and semantic priors into the models, enabling them to perform various 3D tasks. However, the limitations regarding real-world applicability, reliance on SAM, and the lack of more complex tasks prevent it from being considered a truly breakthrough contribution.

Score: 6

**Justification:**

The score of 6 reflects the combination of the paper's strengths and weaknesses. The novelty and potential impact are notable, but the limitations regarding real-world applicability, reliance on a pre-trained model, and the scope of tasks prevent it from achieving a higher score. It presents a valuable incremental contribution to the field of visual prompting for MLLMs and the potential to be expanded upon.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### MAP: Evaluation and Multi-Agent Enhancement of Large Language Models for Inpatient Pathways
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13205v1)
- **Authors**: Zhen Chen, Zhihao Peng, Xusheng Liang, Cheng Wang, Peigan Liang, Linsheng Zeng, Minjie Ju, Yixuan Yuan
- **Abstract**: Inpatient pathways demand complex clinical decision-making based on comprehensive patient information, posing critical challenges for clinicians. Despite advancements in large language models (LLMs) in medical applications, limited research focused on artificial intelligence (AI) inpatient pathways systems, due to the lack of large-scale inpatient datasets. Moreover, existing medical benchmarks typically concentrated on medical question-answering and examinations, ignoring the multifaceted nature of clinical decision-making in inpatient settings. To address these gaps, we first developed the Inpatient Pathway Decision Support (IPDS) benchmark from the MIMIC-IV database, encompassing 51,274 cases across nine triage departments and 17 major disease categories alongside 16 standardized treatment options. Then, we proposed the Multi-Agent Inpatient Pathways (MAP) framework to accomplish inpatient pathways with three clinical agents, including a triage agent managing the patient admission, a diagnosis agent serving as the primary decision maker at the department, and a treatment agent providing treatment plans. Additionally, our MAP framework includes a chief agent overseeing the inpatient pathways to guide and promote these three clinician agents. Extensive experiments showed our MAP improved the diagnosis accuracy by 25.10% compared to the state-of-the-art LLM HuatuoGPT2-13B. It is worth noting that our MAP demonstrated significant clinical compliance, outperforming three board-certified clinicians by 10%-12%, establishing a foundation for inpatient pathways systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the "MAP" (Multi-Agent Inpatient Pathways) framework, designed to improve the performance of large language models (LLMs) in inpatient clinical decision-making. It addresses the limited research in AI systems for inpatient pathways, primarily due to a lack of suitable datasets. To overcome this, the authors develop the "IPDS" (Inpatient Pathway Decision Support) benchmark from the MIMIC-IV database, comprising a large dataset of real patient cases across various departments and disease categories. The MAP framework utilizes three specialized LLM-powered agents (triage, diagnosis, and treatment) collaborating under the guidance of a chief agent. The framework incorporates several modules to enhance performance, including record review, trainable retrieval-enhanced generation (REG), and expert guidance. Experimental results demonstrate that MAP significantly improves diagnostic accuracy compared to existing LLMs and even outperforms board-certified clinicians.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset Contribution:** The creation of the IPDS benchmark is a significant contribution. A substantial and realistically complex dataset like this is critical for the advancement of AI in inpatient care, as previously there has been a lack of suitable resources for this domain.
    *   **Framework Design:** The multi-agent approach, with specialized agents and a chief agent, is a novel and well-structured way to address the complexity of inpatient pathways. This architecture allows for a modular and scalable system, where each agent focuses on a specific task, leading to better overall performance.
    *   **Module Implementation:** The integration of modules like record review, trainable REG, and expert guidance demonstrates careful consideration of the challenges of clinical data analysis and decision-making. The record review module specifically tackles a challenging aspect of noisy or contradictory data. The REG module incorporates structured medical knowledge, and the expert guidance mirrors human clinical supervision, both of which improve clinical compliance.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The thorough experimental results, showing improvements over state-of-the-art LLMs and even outperforming board-certified clinicians, provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the MAP framework. The ablation studies further validate the contribution of each individual module. The statistical validation of clinical decision support is well done.
    *   **Clinical Relevance:** The paper focuses on a highly relevant and impactful problem: improving clinical decision-making in inpatient pathways, where errors can have severe consequences. The system aims to address this issue by providing decision support, increasing compliance, and potentially reducing diagnostic errors.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **MIMIC-IV Limitations:** While MIMIC-IV is a valuable resource, it's important to acknowledge its limitations. The dataset is from a single healthcare system and may not be fully representative of all patient populations or clinical practices. This could limit the generalizability of the MAP framework.
    *   **Lack of Evaluation with other open LLMs:** The authors stated they cannot evaluate Chat GPT, GPT-4, Med-PALM due to a data agreement preventing its data to be used with external APIs.
    *   **Explainability:** While the paper highlights explainable and clinically relevant diagnostic support, there is little to see or infer.
    *   **Ethical Considerations:** The paper addresses de-identification and access guidelines, but a deeper discussion of ethical considerations related to deploying AI systems in healthcare would be valuable. Topics like potential bias in the algorithms, patient privacy, and the role of human oversight should be discussed in greater detail.
    *   **Real-world Validation:** The evaluation is primarily based on a benchmark dataset. Real-world validation in a clinical setting is needed to fully assess the feasibility and impact of the MAP framework.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   **Dataset:** It is significant for the development of novel systems and methods to address AI in inpatient clinical decision making.
    *   **Framework:** The architecture and modules of the MAP framework are original and have the potential to influence future research in this area.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field by addressing a challenging problem with a novel and well-designed framework and a valuable benchmark dataset. The MAP framework shows promise in improving clinical decision-making in inpatient pathways. However, the limitations of the MIMIC-IV dataset and the need for real-world validation temper its potential impact. Also, more attention could be given to ethical considerations and limitations of the proposed MAP framework.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Improving Complex Reasoning with Dynamic Prompt Corruption: A soft prompt Optimization Approach
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13208v1)
- **Authors**: Sinan Fan, Liang Xie, Chen Shen, Ge Teng, Xiaosong Yuan, Xiaofeng Zhang, Chenxi Huang, Wenxiao Wang, Xiaofei He, Jieping Ye
- **Abstract**: Prompt-tuning (PT) for large language models (LLMs) can facilitate the performance on various conventional NLP tasks with significantly fewer trainable parameters. However, our investigation reveals that PT provides limited improvement and may even degrade the primitive performance of LLMs on complex reasoning tasks. Such a phenomenon suggests that soft prompts can positively impact certain instances while negatively affecting others, particularly during the later phases of reasoning. To address these challenges, We first identify an information accumulation within the soft prompts.
