Through detailed analysis, we demonstrate that this phenomenon is often accompanied by erroneous information flow patterns in the deeper layers of the model, which ultimately lead to incorrect reasoning outcomes. we propose a novel method called \textbf{D}ynamic \textbf{P}rompt \textbf{C}orruption (DPC) to take better advantage of soft prompts in complex reasoning tasks, which dynamically adjusts the influence of soft prompts based on their impact on the reasoning process. Specifically, DPC consists of two stages: Dynamic Trigger and Dynamic Corruption. First, Dynamic Trigger measures the impact of soft prompts, identifying whether beneficial or detrimental. Then, Dynamic Corruption mitigates the negative effects of soft prompts by selectively masking key tokens that interfere with the reasoning process. We validate the proposed approach through extensive experiments on various LLMs and reasoning tasks, including GSM8K, MATH, and AQuA. Experimental results demonstrate that DPC can consistently enhance the performance of PT, achieving 4\%-8\% accuracy gains compared to vanilla prompt tuning, highlighting the effectiveness of our approach and its potential to enhance complex reasoning in LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of limited performance or performance degradation of prompt-tuning (PT) on complex reasoning tasks in large language models (LLMs). The authors identify that soft prompts can have both positive and negative effects, with negative impacts often stemming from erroneous information flow patterns and "information accumulation" within soft prompts during later stages of reasoning. To mitigate this, they propose Dynamic Prompt Corruption (DPC), a two-stage method. The first stage, Dynamic Trigger, measures the impact of soft prompts and identifies whether they are beneficial or detrimental. The second stage, Dynamic Corruption, selectively masks key tokens that interfere with the reasoning process.  Experiments on various LLMs and reasoning tasks (GSM8K, MATH, AQUA) demonstrate that DPC consistently enhances PT performance, achieving accuracy gains compared to vanilla prompt tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of dynamically adjusting the influence of soft prompts based on their impact is somewhat novel. The identification of "information accumulation" in soft prompts and its relation to erroneous reasoning is a valuable insight.  The DPC method itself, while using masking techniques, is a relatively straightforward application of these insights. Prior work has explored soft prompts more broadly (Shi and Lipani, 2024), and the attention calibration methods (like ACT) address related but distinct issues. So the contribution lies mostly in identifying and addressing the specific problem related to **later stages of reasoning** and providing a **dynamic** adaptation of prompt tuning.

* **Significance:**  Improving the performance of prompt-tuning on complex reasoning tasks is a significant goal. PT is a parameter-efficient approach, making it valuable for resource-constrained scenarios. The paper demonstrates consistent improvements over vanilla prompt tuning, suggesting a practical approach to improving LLM performance on difficult problems. While the accuracy increases are notable (4-8%), the overall performance on very challenging tasks like MATH remains relatively low, pointing to limitations.  The ablation studies help to understand the contribution of each component of the proposed method, increasing the paper's significance.

* **Strengths:**
    * The paper presents a well-defined problem and clear motivation.
    * The "information accumulation" and error analysis, validated with attention scores are a strength.
    * The method is relatively simple to implement.
    * The experiments cover a range of LLMs and reasoning tasks.
    * The ablation study is informative.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * The DPC method, while effective, is not extremely sophisticated. Masking tokens is a relatively common technique.
    * The improvement, while consistent, isn't always dramatic (especially on the most challenging tasks).
    *  The paper could provide a more thorough comparison to existing methods for improving reasoning capabilities, particularly attention-based and calibration techniques and existing prompt engineering methods designed for CoT reasoning.  Specifically, the reasons *why* ACT doesn't work well for this task, even though they are conceptually related, should be explored more thoroughly.
    * Some implementation details are lacking.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on prompt engineering and parameter-efficient fine-tuning for complex reasoning.  The insights about information flow and the dynamic adjustment approach could inspire new methods. However, the relatively modest performance gains and simplicity of the technique may limit its immediate widespread adoption. The paper motivates further exploration of methods to dynamically regulate prompt influence.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and practical, although relatively straightforward, solution to a specific problem in prompt tuning. The identification of the information accumulation problem is a solid contribution. The experimental results demonstrate consistent improvement, adding to the paper's value. However, the method's simplicity and the modest gains on challenging tasks, combined with the lack of very detailed comparison to related work, limit its overall significance and impact. Therefore, it's not a highly groundbreaking contribution, but a solid improvement over existing prompt-tuning methods for complex reasoning, and useful for future research in the area.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### MedLoRD: A Medical Low-Resource Diffusion Model for High-Resolution 3D CT Image Synthesis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13211v1)
- **Authors**: Marvin Seyfarth, Salman Ul Hassan Dar, Isabelle Ayx, Matthias Alexander Fink, Stefan O. Schoenberg, Hans-Ulrich Kauczor, Sandy Engelhardt
- **Abstract**: Advancements in AI for medical imaging offer significant potential. However, their applications are constrained by the limited availability of data and the reluctance of medical centers to share it due to patient privacy concerns. Generative models present a promising solution by creating synthetic data as a substitute for real patient data. However, medical images are typically high-dimensional, and current state-of-the-art methods are often impractical for computational resource-constrained healthcare environments. These models rely on data sub-sampling, raising doubts about their feasibility and real-world applicability. Furthermore, many of these models are evaluated on quantitative metrics that alone can be misleading in assessing the image quality and clinical meaningfulness of the generated images. To address this, we introduce MedLoRD, a generative diffusion model designed for computational resource-constrained environments. MedLoRD is capable of generating high-dimensional medical volumes with resolutions up to 512$\times$512$\times$256, utilizing GPUs with only 24GB VRAM, which are commonly found in standard desktop workstations. MedLoRD is evaluated across multiple modalities, including Coronary Computed Tomography Angiography and Lung Computed Tomography datasets. Extensive evaluations through radiological evaluation, relative regional volume analysis, adherence to conditional masks, and downstream tasks show that MedLoRD generates high-fidelity images closely adhering to segmentation mask conditions, surpassing the capabilities of current state-of-the-art generative models for medical image synthesis in computational resource-constrained environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MedLoRD, a medical low-resource diffusion model designed for synthesizing high-resolution 3D CT images. It addresses the challenges of limited data availability and computational resource constraints in medical imaging AI. MedLoRD utilizes VQ-VAE GANs for encoding and a 3D U-Net for image denoising in the latent space. The model is specifically designed to operate effectively on standard desktop workstations with limited GPU memory (24GB VRAM). The paper evaluates MedLoRD on coronary CT angiography and lung CT datasets, comparing it against state-of-the-art generative models using radiological evaluation, regional volume analysis, adherence to conditional masks, and downstream tasks. The results indicate that MedLoRD generates high-fidelity images adhering to segmentation mask conditions, surpassing the capabilities of existing models in resource-constrained environments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Resource efficiency:** The primary strength of the paper is its focus on computational resource constraints, a significant barrier to adoption in many clinical settings. The development of a generative model capable of producing high-resolution medical images on relatively modest hardware is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper goes beyond standard quantitative metrics like FID and includes radiological evaluation, regional volume analysis, and downstream task performance. This multi-faceted approach provides a more clinically relevant assessment of image quality and utility.
    *   **Conditional synthesis:** MedLoRD's ability to perform conditional synthesis, generating images based on segmentation masks, enhances its practical applicability in various clinical scenarios.
    *   **Clear articulation of challenges:** The introduction accurately outlines the limitations of existing generative models in the context of medical imaging. The paper explicitly addresses the tension between high dimensionality, resource limitations, and the need for clinically meaningful images.
    *   **Transparency:** The paper provides details on the implementation, datasets, and training procedures, enabling reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited novelty in architecture:** The core architecture (VQ-VAE GAN with a U-Net) is not entirely novel. The paper's main contribution lies in its optimization for resource constraints and comprehensive evaluation, rather than a fundamentally new architectural innovation.
    *   **Dataset size:** The PCCTA dataset size is relatively small (136 volumes, with only 16 used for testing). This raises concerns about the generalizability of the results and the potential for overfitting.
    *   **Conditional Control Mechanism:** In contrast to typical ControlNets, the authors initialize from scratch and reduce the number of channels. This could imply potential limitations in representation learning or generative capabilities.
    *   **Metric limitations:** The paper acknowledges the limitations of relying solely on FID but still includes it. While the authors provide a compelling argument for its inadequacy, the inclusion of a problematic metric can be confusing.
    *   **Downstream task**: While helpful, downstream task performance could vary significantly across other imaging related task such as disease detection or multi-organ segmentation.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper addresses a critical gap in the field by focusing on resource-constrained medical image synthesis.
    *   The comprehensive evaluation framework sets a higher standard for assessing the clinical relevance of generative models in medical imaging.
    *   The development of MedLoRD provides a practical tool for generating synthetic medical images, potentially facilitating research and development in resource-limited settings.
    *   The work promotes a more critical perspective on evaluation metrics in generative models, highlighting the importance of clinical context and relevance.

*   **Influence:**

    *   The paper could influence future research by encouraging the development of more resource-efficient generative models for medical imaging.
    *   The evaluation framework could be adopted by other researchers to provide a more rigorous assessment of their models.
    *   The availability of MedLoRD could stimulate research in areas such as data augmentation, privacy-preserving machine learning, and personalized medicine.

**Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7**.

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of medical image synthesis by specifically addressing the practical challenges of computational resource constraints and evaluation. While the core architectural components are not entirely novel, the optimization for resource efficiency and the comprehensive evaluation framework are significant strengths. The paper is well-written, clearly articulates the challenges, and provides detailed information about the methods and results. The relatively small size of the PCCTA dataset and some architectural decisions (such as the initialization of controlnet) raise some concerns about generalizability, and the heavy reliance on MAISIPT as a baseline which already demonstrated comparable results also limits the significance of the findings, preventing a higher score. However, the focus on practical applicability and the thorough evaluation approach make it a worthwhile contribution.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MAME: Multidimensional Adaptive Metamer Exploration with Human Perceptual Feedback
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13212v1)
- **Authors**: Mina Kamao, Hayato Ono, Ayumu Yamashita, Kaoru Amano, Masataka Sawayama
- **Abstract**: Alignment between human brain networks and artificial models is actively studied in machine learning and neuroscience. A widely adopted approach to explore their functional alignment is to identify metamers for both humans and models. Metamers refer to input stimuli that are physically different but equivalent within a given system. If a model's metameric space completely matched the human metameric space, the model would achieve functional alignment with humans. However, conventional methods lack direct ways to search for human metamers. Instead, researchers first develop biologically inspired models and then infer about human metamers indirectly by testing whether model metamers also appear as metamers to humans. Here, we propose the Multidimensional Adaptive Metamer Exploration (MAME) framework, enabling direct high-dimensional exploration of human metameric space. MAME leverages online image generation guided by human perceptual feedback. Specifically, it modulates reference images across multiple dimensions by leveraging hierarchical responses from convolutional neural networks (CNNs). Generated images are presented to participants whose perceptual discriminability is assessed in a behavioral task. Based on participants' responses, subsequent image generation parameters are adaptively updated online. Using our MAME framework, we successfully measured a human metameric space of over fifty dimensions within a single experiment. Experimental results showed that human discrimination sensitivity was lower for metameric images based on low-level features compared to high-level features, which image contrast metrics could not explain. The finding suggests that the model computes low-level information not essential for human perception. Our framework has the potential to contribute to developing interpretable AI and understanding of brain function in neuroscience.
- **Summary**: Okay, let's break down this paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework called Multidimensional Adaptive Metamer Exploration (MAME) for directly exploring human metameric spaces, i.e., spaces of images that humans perceive as similar despite physical differences.  Unlike traditional methods that rely on pre-existing, biologically-inspired models to generate metamers and then test their perceptual equivalence, MAME utilizes online image generation guided by real-time human perceptual feedback. It leverages convolutional neural networks (CNNs) and independent component analysis (ICA) to define a multidimensional search space and adaptively updates image generation parameters based on participant responses in an ABX task (a discrimination task). The paper demonstrates the framework's ability to measure a high-dimensional metameric space, revealing discrepancies between human perception and CNN representations, particularly at lower levels of visual processing (specifically, in CNN's conv1 features).  The authors argue that MAME facilitates a more direct and flexible approach to understanding human vision and functional alignment with AI models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The core idea of directly exploring human metameric space *using human feedback to guide the search* is a definite step forward compared to the established paradigm. The approach mitigates reliance on pre-aligned models, a significant constraint in previous work. The combination of CNN feature extraction, ICA for dimensionality reduction, and online image generation guided by ABX task feedback is a technically novel integration of existing techniques.

**Significance:** The findings suggest that low-level features extracted by CNNs might not be as perceptually relevant to humans as their higher-level features. If validated further, this can guide the development of more human-aligned AI models. The framework has the potential to be applied to various cognitive neuroscience and AI alignment problems. Additionally, the demonstrated capacity to efficiently explore high-dimensional perceptual spaces directly opens possibilities for investigating other sensory modalities or cognitive processes. The authors also clearly articulate potential improvements by exploring different metameric equivalence conditions and models.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** The core idea of MAME addresses a clear limitation in the existing metamer generation literature.
*   **Technical Integration:** The framework demonstrates a successful combination of diverse techniques to achieve its goal.
*   **Clear Experimental Design:**  The ABX task and the staircase method are well-established psychophysical techniques appropriately used.
*   **Interesting Initial Results:** The findings regarding discrepancies between human perception and CNNs at different levels of processing are intriguing and warrant further investigation.
*   **Clear Limitations and Future Directions:** The authors clearly acknowledge the limitations of their chosen parameter space and possible ways forward for future works.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of Exploration:** While claiming to explore a high-dimensional space, the experiment is limited to 54 dimensions based on a specific pre-selected CNN architecture (robust ResNet50), three layers, and a few ICA components. Using a different CNN architecture or exploring more layers/components could provide a very different result.
*   **Dependence on CNNs (even for Direction):** Although the authors claim to relax the dependence on perfect models, the *exploration direction* is still defined by the CNN feature space. While it's a weaker dependence than *generating* the metamers entirely from a model, it still biases the search.
*   **Gram Matrix Limitations:**  The use of Gram matrices, while efficient for texture representation, discards spatial information, which may influence the types of metamers found and limit the applicability to some visual tasks.
*   **Modest Subject Sample:** Eight participants is sufficient for a basic psychophysical study, but replicating the results with larger and more diverse samples would enhance confidence.
*   **Somewhat Incremental Advance:**  While the framework *is* novel in its specific combination, the individual components (CNN feature extraction, ICA, ABX tasks) are well-established. The contribution is primarily in *how* these elements are integrated.
*   **No Direct Comparison:** The paper lacks direct comparison of MAME's performance with a traditional metamer generation pipeline.

**Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in AI alignment, perceptual modeling, and computational neuroscience. The MAME framework provides a valuable tool for probing human perception and can be extended to other sensory domains. The findings on human-CNN alignment, while preliminary, can motivate the design of more perceptually aligned AI models.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid and novel framework for exploring human metameric spaces. The key innovation is the use of online, human-guided exploration, which is a significant improvement over traditional model-dependent approaches. The initial findings highlight important discrepancies between human and CNN perception, offering valuable insights for AI alignment. However, the reliance on CNN feature space for exploration direction, limited exploration scope, and the somewhat incremental nature of the technical contribution prevent a higher score. I think that this study is likely to be cited well, and I look forward to the results of future studies using this framework.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Can Language Models Follow Multiple Turns of Entangled Instructions?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13222v1)
- **Authors**: Chi Han
- **Abstract**: Despite significant achievements in improving the instruction-following capabilities of large language models (LLMs), the ability to process multiple potentially entangled or conflicting instructions remains a considerable challenge. Real-world scenarios often require consistency across multiple instructions over time, such as secret privacy, personal preferences, and prioritization, which demand sophisticated abilities to integrate multiple turns and carefully balance competing objectives when instructions intersect or conflict. This work presents a systematic investigation of LLMs' capabilities in handling multiple turns of instructions, covering three levels of difficulty: (1) retrieving information from instructions, (2) tracking and reasoning across turns, and (3) resolving conflicts among instructions. We construct MultiTurnInstruct with around 1.1K high-quality multi-turn conversations through the human-in-the-loop approach and result in nine capability categories, including statics and dynamics, reasoning, and multitasking. Our finding reveals an intriguing trade-off between different capabilities. While GPT models demonstrate superior memorization, they show reduced effectiveness in privacy-protection tasks requiring selective information withholding. Larger models exhibit stronger reasoning capabilities but still struggle with resolving conflicting instructions. Importantly, these performance gaps cannot be attributed solely to information loss, as models demonstrate strong BLEU scores on memorization tasks but their attention mechanisms fail to integrate multiple related instructions effectively. These findings highlight critical areas for improvement in complex real-world tasks involving multi-turn instructions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Can Language Models Follow Multiple Turns of Entangled Instructions?":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to handle multiple turns of instruction, focusing on scenarios where instructions are entangled, conflicting, or require reasoning across multiple turns.  The authors introduce MULTITURNINSTRUCT, a new dataset consisting of ~1.1K multi-turn conversations designed to assess LLMs on three levels of complexity: (1) retrieving information, (2) tracking and reasoning across turns, and (3) resolving instruction conflicts. The dataset covers nine different capabilities, including statics/dynamics, reasoning, and multitasking. The authors' findings reveal an interesting trade-off: models strong in memorization may struggle with selective information withholding, and larger models with reasoning capabilities may falter at resolving conflicting instructions. The paper highlights that these limitations aren't solely due to information loss but also stem from the attention mechanisms' inability to effectively integrate related instructions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary strength lies in its dataset, MULTITURNINSTRUCT, which explicitly targets the multi-turn instruction-following capabilities of LLMs in complex scenarios with entangled or conflicting instructions. This is a valuable contribution as many existing datasets focus on single-turn instructions or multi-turn interactions with limited interdependency between turns. It moves beyond just "stacking" instructions.  The focus on different *levels* of difficulty also adds nuance. The attention map analysis further attempts to dissect *why* models fail on certain task.
*   **Significance:**  The paper's findings have practical implications for the deployment of LLMs in real-world applications. By identifying specific weaknesses in handling multiple, complex instructions, it highlights crucial areas for improvement.  The trade-offs observed between different capabilities (e.g., memorization vs. privacy) are particularly important for developers to consider. This work pushes the field beyond simply measuring instruction-following and toward a deeper understanding of the underlying mechanisms and limitations. It could influence future research directions in pre-training, fine-tuning, and attention mechanism design.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The MULTITURNINSTRUCT dataset fills a gap in the existing resources for evaluating LLMs.
    *   The paper systematically analyzes LLM performance across different levels of complexity and capabilities.
    *   The identification of trade-offs provides valuable insights for practical LLM development.
    *   The analysis of attention patterns offers a glimpse into the reasons behind model failures.
    *   The rule-based dataset curation mitigates bias

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the dataset is valuable, the size of ~1.1K dialogues might be considered modest. More data in each category could lead to more statistically robust findings.
    *   The evaluation is primarily based on automatic metrics (BLEU, exact match). While these provide a good starting point, human evaluation could offer more qualitative insights into the quality of the responses.
    *   The analysis of attention patterns, while interesting, is limited to a few examples. A more comprehensive analysis across the entire dataset could strengthen the conclusions.
    *   The paper doesn't delve into specific architectural modifications or training strategies that could address the identified limitations.

*   **Overall:** The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by introducing a targeted dataset and systematically evaluating LLM performance on complex, multi-turn instruction-following tasks. The identification of trade-offs and the analysis of attention patterns provide valuable insights for future research and development. While there's room for improvement in terms of dataset size, evaluation metrics, and prescriptive solutions, the paper is a valuable step forward in understanding the limitations of current LLMs and guiding future advancements.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper warrants a 7.5 because it's a well-executed and insightful study that directly addresses a relevant problem in LLM research. The novel dataset fills a critical gap. The experimental design is sound.  However, it doesn't present any fundamentally new *techniques* for improving LLMs, the analysis is somewhat preliminary in terms of dissecting *why* certain results were obtained, and the dataset size is not substantial. Also, it's a *benchmark* paper, which tends to have less impact than one that proposes/proves something.  The paper is valuable but would have benefited from exploring mitigation strategies, or more thoroughly examining attention. Therefore, the paper is placed above average with a 7.5.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### TablePilot; Recommending Human-Preferred Tabular Data Analysis with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13262v1)
- **Authors**: Deyin Yi, Yihao Liu, Lang Cao, Mengyu Zhou, Haoyu Dong, Shi Han, Dongmei Zhang
- **Abstract**: Tabular data analysis is crucial in many scenarios, yet efficiently identifying the most relevant data analysis queries and results for a new table remains a significant challenge. The complexity of tabular data, diverse analytical operations, and the demand for high-quality analysis make the process tedious. To address these challenges, we aim to recommend query-code-result triplets tailored for new tables in tabular data analysis workflows. In this paper, we present TablePilot, a pioneering tabular data analysis framework leveraging large language models to autonomously generate comprehensive and superior analytical results without relying on user profiles or prior interactions. The framework incorporates key designs in analysis preparation and analysis optimization to enhance accuracy. Additionally, we propose Rec-Align, a novel method to further improve recommendation quality and better align with human preferences. Experiments on DART, a dataset specifically designed for comprehensive tabular data analysis recommendation, demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework. Based on GPT-4o, the tuned TablePilot achieves 77.0% top-5 recommendation recall. Human evaluations further highlight its effectiveness in optimizing tabular data analysis workflows.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper, followed by a score and justification:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TablePilot, a framework designed to recommend human-preferred tabular data analysis queries, code, and results in a zero-turn setting. It utilizes large language models (LLMs) to autonomously generate analytical results without relying on user profiles or historical interactions.  The framework incorporates analysis preparation steps (sampling and explanation), module-based analysis (basic analysis, visualization, statistical modeling), and analysis optimization. A key contribution is Rec-Align, a method for aligning recommendations with human preferences through a ranking model trained with Direct Preference Optimization (DPO).  The authors also contribute a new dataset, DART, for evaluating tabular data analysis recommendation. Experiments demonstrate that TablePilot achieves improved recall and human preference alignment compared to baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel application of LLMs to the problem of tabular data analysis recommendation in a zero-turn setting. While previous work has explored tabular data analysis and LLMs separately, TablePilot integrates them in a complete framework. The integration of Rec-Align using DPO is also a notable contribution, as it directly addresses the challenge of aligning LLM-generated analysis with human preferences.

*   **Significance:** The problem of efficient tabular data analysis is crucial across many domains. The paper's approach addresses key challenges, including the complexity of tabular data, diverse analytical operations, and the need for high-quality analysis results. It offers a potentially automated method for enhancing human data analysis workflows. The demonstration of its improvement using human evaluation proves its significance in improving analytical data analysis workflows.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation.
    *   Comprehensive framework with distinct stages.
    *   Effective integration of LLMs and preference learning (DPO).
    *   New dataset (DART) addressing limitations of existing resources.
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating performance improvements and human preference alignment.
    *   Extensive ablation studies showing the value of different framework components.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the zero-turn setting is valuable, it also might limit potential performance gains that personalized recommendations can offer, which is a common consideration in most real-world use-cases.
    *   The DART dataset, although a contribution, could be expanded in scale, diversity, and image contrast, and have better-annotated high-quality samples for fine-tuning tasks.
    *   While human evaluation is included, more extensive user studies could provide further validation of the framework's real-world applicability and long-term benefits.
    *   Computational efficiency wasn't adequately discussed. The process mentioned in the paper of calling multiple LLMs may induce efficiency issues.

*   **Potential Influence:** TablePilot has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical framework for automating tabular data analysis recommendation using LLMs. It offers a new direction in aligning LLM-generated results with human preferences. The release of the DART dataset may further encourage research in this area.

*   **Score:** 7.5

*   **Justification:** The paper demonstrates a strong application of LLMs to a relevant and challenging problem in data analysis. The framework is well-designed, and the results demonstrate tangible improvements in accuracy and human preference alignment. The authors created a new dataset for the task. However, limitations in the dataset scale, image contrast, and the scope of user studies are worth mentioning. The model is also computationally expensive and should be noted.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### FlexWorld: Progressively Expanding 3D Scenes for Flexiable-View Synthesis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13265v1)
- **Authors**: Luxi Chen, Zihan Zhou, Min Zhao, Yikai Wang, Ge Zhang, Wenhao Huang, Hao Sun, Ji-Rong Wen, Chongxuan Li
- **Abstract**: Generating flexible-view 3D scenes, including 360{\deg} rotation and zooming, from single images is challenging due to a lack of 3D data. To this end, we introduce FlexWorld, a novel framework consisting of two key components: (1) a strong video-to-video (V2V) diffusion model to generate high-quality novel view images from incomplete input rendered from a coarse scene, and (2) a progressive expansion process to construct a complete 3D scene. In particular, leveraging an advanced pre-trained video model and accurate depth-estimated training pairs, our V2V model can generate novel views under large camera pose variations. Building upon it, FlexWorld progressively generates new 3D content and integrates it into the global scene through geometry-aware scene fusion. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of FlexWorld in generating high-quality novel view videos and flexible-view 3D scenes from single images, achieving superior visual quality under multiple popular metrics and datasets compared to existing state-of-the-art methods. Qualitatively, we highlight that FlexWorld can generate high-fidelity scenes with flexible views like 360{\deg} rotations and zooming. Project page: https://ml-gsai.github.io/FlexWorld.
- **Summary**: **Summary of the Paper:** The paper introduces FlexWorld, a framework aimed at generating flexible-view 3D scenes, enabling actions such as 360-degree rotation and zooming from single images. The framework comprises two main components:  1. A video-to-video (V2V) diffusion model that synthesizes high-quality images from incomplete views of a coarse scene, overcoming challenges typically present due to insufficient 3D data. 2. A progressive expansion process that incrementally builds a complete 3D scene through geometry-aware fusion of new content. The efficacy of FlexWorld is demonstrated through extensive experiments, showcasing its superiority in generating high-fidelity videos and flexible 3D views compared to existing methods across several evaluation metrics and datasets. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper presents a significant advancement in the field of 3D scene generation and flexible-view synthesis. The novelty lies in the integration of a strong V2V diffusion model with a structured approach to scene expansion, both of which leverage pre-trained models and depth estimation techniques. This allows for greater flexibility in rendering novel views from limited data, which has been a significant hurdle in computer vision and graphics. **Strengths:** - **Innovative Approach:** The proposed combination of V2V modeling and geometry-aware scene fusion offers a new perspective in generating 3D scenes, which could inspire further research in related areas. - **Empirical Validation:** The extensive experimental validation against established metrics and existing state-of-the-art methods strengthens the claims of effectiveness and visual quality. - **Practical Application:** The ability to create flexible views from single images has practical implications in fields such as virtual reality, gaming, and digital content creation, thus showcasing the paper's relevance. **Weaknesses:** - **Dependence on Data Quality:** The approach heavily relies on the availability of accurately depth-estimated training pairs, which could limit the applicability in cases where high-quality supervising data is scarce. - **Incremental Contribution:** While it is a solid advancement, the paper does not disrupt the current paradigm significantly—flexible-view synthesis remains a niche with many ongoing efforts, making this work one among many rather than a groundbreaking shift. - **Computational Complexity:** The implementation of a progressive expansion process implies a potentially increased computational cost, which necessitates further exploration on efficiency. **Influence on the Field:** FlexWorld could influence future research directions by emphasizing the importance of hybrid models that synergize different methodologies in 3D space synthesis. However, successful integration and application in varied real-world scenarios would be vital for its broader acceptance. Overall, considering its novel contributions, the rigorous validation, and the practical implications while also balancing the limitations identified, I would assign the paper a score of 8.  **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Generative Gaussian Splatting: Generating 3D Scenes with Video Diffusion Priors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13272v1)
- **Authors**: Katja Schwarz, Norman Mueller, Peter Kontschieder
- **Abstract**: Synthesizing consistent and photorealistic 3D scenes is an open problem in computer vision. Video diffusion models generate impressive videos but cannot directly synthesize 3D representations, i.e., lack 3D consistency in the generated sequences. In addition, directly training generative 3D models is challenging due to a lack of 3D training data at scale. In this work, we present Generative Gaussian Splatting (GGS) -- a novel approach that integrates a 3D representation with a pre-trained latent video diffusion model. Specifically, our model synthesizes a feature field parameterized via 3D Gaussian primitives. The feature field is then either rendered to feature maps and decoded into multi-view images, or directly upsampled into a 3D radiance field. We evaluate our approach on two common benchmark datasets for scene synthesis, RealEstate10K and ScanNet+, and find that our proposed GGS model significantly improves both the 3D consistency of the generated multi-view images, and the quality of the generated 3D scenes over all relevant baselines. Compared to a similar model without 3D representation, GGS improves FID on the generated 3D scenes by ~20% on both RealEstate10K and ScanNet+. Project page: https://katjaschwarz.github.io/ggs/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Generative Gaussian Splatting (GGS), a novel approach for synthesizing 3D scenes from one or more posed reference images. GGS integrates a 3D Gaussian Splatting representation with a pre-trained latent video diffusion model. It generates feature fields parameterized by 3D Gaussians, which are then either rendered into feature maps and decoded into multi-view images, or directly upsampled into a 3D radiance field. The model is trained with v-prediction and additional depth supervision, and is shown to improve both the 3D consistency of generated images and the quality of the generated 3D scenes on RealEstate10K and ScanNet++ datasets. Compared to a similar model without 3D representation, GGS significantly improves FID.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of integrating an explicit 3D representation (Gaussian Splats) with a pre-trained video diffusion model is novel. Most prior work either relies on generating 2D images and then reconstructing 3D or uses custom diffusion model architectures not compatible with pre-trained video models. The approach of learning a feature field representation instead of directly predicting noise in latent space is also a key contribution to solving the incompatibility issue. The custom decoder for predicting the decoded 3D scene directly from feature maps further adds to the novelty.
*   **Significance:** Generating consistent and photorealistic 3D scenes remains a significant challenge. The paper addresses the problem that video diffusion models lack inherent 3D consistency, and generative 3D models suffer from limited training data. By leveraging pre-trained video diffusion models and a 3D representation, GGS achieves state-of-the-art results, particularly in terms of 3D consistency and generation quality. The method has the potential to impact various applications, including novel view synthesis, scene editing, and 3D asset generation.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong integration of 3D representation with pre-trained diffusion models.
    *   Addresses the issue of 3D consistency in generated images.
    *   Demonstrated improvement in both image quality and 3D consistency.
    *   Effective use of Gaussian Splats for fast rendering and numerical stability.
    *   Explicit handling of camera pose as a conditioning factor.
    *   Custom decoder design that improves 3D consistency.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the authors are careful with their baseline comparisons, the strong advantage given by access to a proprietary pretrained 2B parameter diffusion model is difficult to gauge; this raises questions about how much the results depend on this particular starting point,
    *   While the paper does mention the compute required to train their models (8xA100s for a few weeks) this is a huge barrier and needs to be more explicit, since it is significantly more than most academic groups will have access to.
    *   The ablation studies do a decent job of justifying design decisions, but the proprietary backbone adds complexity to the analysis
    *   Some of the qualitative results show that the generated images by ViewCrafter have altered color and deformed objects on ScanNet++ dataset. These artifacts by ViewCrafter suggest ViewCrafter doesn’t generalize well to this dataset and this may lead to unfair conclusion against GGS.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's approach of integrating 3D representations into diffusion models could influence future research directions in generative 3D modeling and novel view synthesis. Other researchers may adopt similar strategies to combine the benefits of powerful pre-trained models and explicit 3D structure. The training tricks and architecture choices outlined in the paper can also be adapted for other 3D generative tasks.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a novel and significant contribution to the field of 3D scene generation by effectively integrating a 3D representation with a powerful pre-trained video diffusion model. The demonstrated improvements in 3D consistency and image quality are noteworthy. Although there are several baselines, there is concern about dependence on access to a specific high-parameter backbone and lack of access and the computational burden of training the model. The design seems solid though, and the experimental evaluation has a couple of concerns, but there are reasonable efforts to control the conclusions. Taking everything together, the paper earns a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLM-Match: An Open-Sourced Patient Matching Model Based on Large Language Models and Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13281v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaodi Li, Shaika Chowdhury, Chung Il Wi, Maria Vassilaki, Ken Liu, Terence T Sio, Owen Garrick, Young J Juhn, James R Cerhan, Cui Tao, Nansu Zong
- **Abstract**: Patient matching is the process of linking patients to appropriate clinical trials by accurately identifying and matching their medical records with trial eligibility criteria. We propose LLM-Match, a novel framework for patient matching leveraging fine-tuned open-source large language models. Our approach consists of four key components. First, a retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) module extracts relevant patient context from a vast pool of electronic health records (EHRs). Second, a prompt generation module constructs input prompts by integrating trial eligibility criteria (both inclusion and exclusion criteria), patient context, and system instructions. Third, a fine-tuning module with a classification head optimizes the model parameters using structured prompts and ground-truth labels. Fourth, an evaluation module assesses the fine-tuned model's performance on the testing datasets. We evaluated LLM-Match on four open datasets, n2c2, SIGIR, TREC 2021, and TREC 2022, using open-source models, comparing it against TrialGPT, Zero-Shot, and GPT-4-based closed models. LLM-Match outperformed all baselines.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided research paper. Here's a summary and a critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM-Match, an open-source patient-trial matching framework leveraging fine-tuned large language models (LLMs) and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG).  LLM-Match consists of four modules: a RAG module for extracting relevant patient information from EHRs, a prompt generation module for constructing structured prompts using eligibility criteria and patient context, a fine-tuning module that optimizes the model with a classification head, and an evaluation module.  The paper evaluates LLM-Match on four open datasets (n2c2, SIGIR, TREC 2021, and TREC 2022), demonstrating its superior performance against baselines, including TrialGPT and zero-shot LLM approaches, especially when using a classification head. The key contribution is a fully open-sourced, customizable, and high-performing framework for patient-trial matching that surpasses proprietary solutions while addressing privacy concerns associated with commercial LLMs. The fine-tuning with a classification head and the use of smaller open-source models are highlighted as key innovations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Open-Source and Privacy-Conscious:** The focus on open-source LLMs (LLaMA 3, DeepSeek) is a significant strength, addressing a major limitation of many existing solutions that rely on proprietary models like GPT-4.  This enhances accessibility, transparency, and reduces reliance on potentially expensive and privacy-compromising commercial APIs.
    *   **Classification Head Enhancement:** The introduction and rigorous evaluation of the classification head within the fine-tuning module are a clear technical contribution. The ablation studies convincingly demonstrate its positive impact on model performance across multiple datasets, enhancing the model's decision-making capabilities in eligibility prediction. The data shows an ability to increase the macro-F1 measure, leading to better ability to detect edge-cases.
    *   **RAG Integration:** The combination of RAG with fine-tuning is well-motivated and addresses the critical challenge of processing large volumes of unstructured EHR data efficiently. RAG filters relevant patient information.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation on diverse benchmark datasets, using relevant metrics like Macro-F1 and AUROC, which strengthens the validity of the findings. The datasets are common clinical trial datasets.
    *   **Superior performance to alternatives:** The paper convincingly shows better performance than SOTA baselines across several datasets.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Data Limitation:** The primary weakness is the use of synthetic datasets, which are inherently limited in their ability to represent the complexities and nuances of real-world EHR data, though the n2c2 datasets are noted as being closer to the real world. The paper acknowledges this limitation, however.
    *   **Simplified Prompt Engineering:** While the paper describes prompt generation, the details of the actual prompt templates used are somewhat limited. The paper does not adequately address the optimization of the prompts.
    *   **General Fine-Tuning Process:** The fine-tuning section mentions the potential improvements of using reinforcement learning methods to further enhance decision-making capabilities. This is a point that can be enhanced in the future.
    *   **Noise reduction:** The authors acknowledge that the prompt generation module concatenates EHRs during prompt generation, which may introduce noise and reduce performance. Further research and study is needed to understand how to improve that.
    *   **Time Dependency and Limited Token Size:**  The paper acknowledges not accounting for time-dependent factors and relatively small token size. This is a limitation that may not make the framework realistic for real-world use.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   **Novelty:** While RAG and LLMs have been applied to healthcare, the *specific* combination of RAG with fine-tuning *and* a classification head for patient-trial matching using open-source models is reasonably novel. The detailed analysis of the impact of the classification head contributes to the understanding of how to best leverage LLMs for this task. The focus on creating a fully open-sourced pipeline differentiates it from other work.
    *   **Significance:**  The paper has the potential to significantly impact clinical trial recruitment by providing a more accessible, scalable, and transparent solution. By addressing privacy concerns and demonstrating competitive performance with open-source models, LLM-Match could democratize access to AI-driven patient-trial matching, benefiting researchers, clinicians, and patients.

**Justification of Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7**. The paper presents a well-executed and valuable contribution to the field of clinical trial recruitment. The strengths in open-sourcing, the classification head addition, RAG integration, and comprehensive evaluation are substantial. However, the synthetic data limitation is a significant concern that tempers the overall impact. The score reflects the potential influence of the research, balanced by the need for further validation on real-world datasets and further research that can improve performance of the framework. The limitations of the prompt engineering process and the time dependency factors require additional study.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 0/10

### A Survey on Transformer Context Extension: Approaches and Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13299v1)
- **Authors**: Yijun Liu, Jinzheng Yu, Yang Xu, Zhongyang Li, Qingfu Zhu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) based on Transformer have been widely applied in the filed of natural language processing (NLP), demonstrating strong performance, particularly in handling short text tasks. However, when it comes to long context scenarios, the performance of LLMs degrades due to some challenges. To alleviate this phenomenon, there is a number of work proposed recently. In this survey, we first list the challenges of applying pre-trained LLMs to process long contexts. Then systematically review the approaches related to long context and propose our taxonomy categorizing them into four main types: positional encoding, context compression, retrieval augmented, and attention pattern. In addition to the approaches, we focus on the evaluation of long context, organizing relevant data, tasks, and metrics based on existing long context benchmarks. Finally, we summarize unresolved issues in the long context domain and put forward our views on future developments.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper is a survey on techniques for extending the context length of transformer-based large language models (LLMs).  It identifies three key challenges: out-of-distribution (OOD) problems, the "Lost in the Middle" phenomenon, and quadratic complexity of attention. The survey proposes a novel taxonomy to categorize existing approaches into four main groups: positional encoding, context compression, retrieval augmentation, and attention pattern modifications. Furthermore, it comprehensively organizes the evaluation aspect, classifying relevant data, tasks, and metrics used in long context benchmarks. Finally, the survey discusses open problems and future research directions in the field.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's primary novelty lies in its proposed taxonomy for organizing the various context extension techniques. While individual methods are not new, the categorization provides a structured way to understand and compare them. The focus on both *approaches* and *evaluation* is a strength, setting it apart from more narrowly focused surveys. However, some overlap between categories is acknowledged, potentially weakening the distinctiveness of the taxonomy.

*   **Significance:** Addressing the limitations of LLMs in long context scenarios is crucial for a wide range of applications (e.g., document processing, dialogue systems, code generation). By systematically reviewing and categorizing the existing approaches and evaluation methodologies, the survey serves as a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners working in this domain. Its identification of open problems and future directions can help guide future research efforts. However, the survey acknowledges that most current work doesn't directly address the core challenges, reducing its immediate impact on solving them.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive coverage of both approaches and evaluation.
    *   Well-structured and organized presentation.
    *   Clear identification of key challenges.
    *   The taxonomy offers a valuable framework for understanding the field.
    *   Includes future research directions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Acknowledged overlap within the proposed taxonomy.
    *   Most of the evaluated techniques do not directly address the fundamental issues of OOD, lost in the middle or quadratic complexity.
    *   The survey is time-sensitive; the rapid development in the field means it may quickly become outdated.
    *   Relies on the survey paper, so may be biased, even if unintentionally.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field by providing researchers with a structured understanding of the various approaches and evaluation methods used for context extension in LLMs. This clarity can lead to more focused and effective research efforts, ultimately accelerating the development of more capable and efficient long context LLMs.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper offers a significant contribution through its comprehensive categorization of context extension techniques and the structured organization of data, tasks and metrics for evaluation. The taxonomy, although not without limitations, provides a valuable framework for understanding the field. The paper's identification of core challenges and future research directions is also beneficial. However, the high score is tempered by the acknowledgements of overlapping areas of taxonomy, and the lack of specific analysis for the underlying identified OOD, lost in middle and complexity issues. The reliance on the survey paper is a further, minor limitation.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Computation Mechanism Behind LLM Position Generalization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13305v1)
- **Authors**: Chi Han, Heng Ji
- **Abstract**: Most written natural languages are composed of sequences of words and sentences. Similar to humans, large language models (LLMs) exhibit flexibility in handling textual positions - a phenomenon we term position generalization. They can understand texts with position perturbations and generalize to longer texts than those encountered during training with the latest techniques. These phenomena suggest that LLMs handle positions tolerantly, but how LLMs computationally process positional relevance remains largely unexplored. This work connects the linguistic phenomenon with LLMs' computational mechanisms. We show how LLMs enforce certain computational mechanisms for the aforementioned tolerance in position perturbations. Despite the complex design of the self-attention mechanism, this work reveals that LLMs learn a counterintuitive disentanglement of attention logits. Their values show a 0.959 linear correlation with an approximation of the arithmetic sum of positional relevance and semantic importance. Furthermore, we identify a prevalent pattern in intermediate features, which we prove theoretically enables this effect. The pattern, which is different from how randomly initialized parameters would behave, suggests that it is a learned behavior rather than a natural result of the model architecture. Based on these findings, we provide computational explanations and criteria for LLMs' position flexibilities. This work takes a pioneering step in linking position generalization with modern LLMs' internal mechanisms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the computational mechanisms behind position generalization in Large Language Models (LLMs). It argues that LLMs exhibit tolerance to position perturbations and generalize to longer sequences due to a counter-intuitive disentanglement of attention logits. Specifically, the paper claims that attention logits can be approximated as the sum of positional relevance (dependent on token distance) and semantic importance (dependent on token semantics). Through empirical analysis, the paper identifies a prevalent pattern in intermediate features that enables this disentanglement. By connecting a linguistic phenomenon to LLMs' computational mechanisms, the work provides computational explanations and criteria for LLMs' position flexibilities. The paper then shows how this disentanglement allows for tolerance of word order perturbations and generalization to longer sequences by maintaining the distribution of the attention outputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of disentangling positional and semantic information in LLMs' attention mechanism is relatively novel. While previous work has explored positional encodings and their impact, this paper focuses on the *computational consequences* of those encodings and reveals how LLMs *learn* to handle position. The link between the low-rank property of attention logit matrices and this disentanglement also appears to be new.

*   **Significance:** If the findings are robust and generalizable, this work has several significant implications. First, it provides a better understanding of the inner workings of LLMs, addressing the critical issue of interpretability. Second, it potentially offers a way to improve LLMs' robustness and generalization capabilities by explicitly encouraging the disentanglement of positional and semantic information during training. Third, the observed special pattern in RoPE-based attention and its theoretical support may inform future architecture designs to promote position flexibility.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper addresses an important and well-defined problem: understanding how LLMs achieve position generalization.
    *   **Interesting Empirical Findings:** The observation of low-rank components in attention logits and the disentanglement of positional and semantic information are compelling. The relatively simple model to fit the logit map is surprising and significant.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The theoretical analysis provides a mathematical explanation for the empirical findings, increasing the credibility of the work.
    *   **Connection to Linguistic Phenomena:** The paper links the computational mechanisms to observed linguistic phenomena like tolerance to word order permutations, which strengthens the impact and relevance of the research.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluations:** The paper includes evaluations for both the perplexity and downstream tasks.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The paper mainly focuses on RoPE and Llama models, and may need further research and analysis for other architectures and models. The investigation of other possible factors for improving the robustness or reducing perplexity is also limited.
    *   **Simplifications:** The theoretical analysis relies on certain assumptions about the distribution of key and query vectors, which might not hold perfectly in practice. The use of asymptotic analysis also introduces limitations.
    *   **Causality:** While the paper shows a correlation between disentanglement and position generalization, it does not definitively prove that disentanglement *causes* position generalization. It is possible that other factors are at play.
    *   **Generality of the findings:** The findings may be specific to the types of tasks and data used. Do these disentangling phenomena hold for multimodal LLMs or LLMs trained on data from different domains?

*   **Justification:**

    This paper presents a valuable, novel analysis of how LLMs process positional information. The findings are well-supported by both empirical results and theoretical justification, demonstrating significant effort and rigor. While the paper has certain limitations in scope and the need for more exploration, the potential impact on LLM research and development makes it a significant contribution. Its impact is, however, dampened by its current focus on RoPE, a single positional embedding technique.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Edit Transfer: Learning Image Editing via Vision In-Context Relations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13327v1)
- **Authors**: Lan Chen, Qi Mao, Yuchao Gu, Mike Zheng Shou
- **Abstract**: We introduce a new setting, Edit Transfer, where a model learns a transformation from just a single source-target example and applies it to a new query image. While text-based methods excel at semantic manipulations through textual prompts, they often struggle with precise geometric details (e.g., poses and viewpoint changes). Reference-based editing, on the other hand, typically focuses on style or appearance and fails at non-rigid transformations. By explicitly learning the editing transformation from a source-target pair, Edit Transfer mitigates the limitations of both text-only and appearance-centric references. Drawing inspiration from in-context learning in large language models, we propose a visual relation in-context learning paradigm, building upon a DiT-based text-to-image model. We arrange the edited example and the query image into a unified four-panel composite, then apply lightweight LoRA fine-tuning to capture complex spatial transformations from minimal examples. Despite using only 42 training samples, Edit Transfer substantially outperforms state-of-the-art TIE and RIE methods on diverse non-rigid scenarios, demonstrating the effectiveness of few-shot visual relation learning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Edit Transfer: Learning Image Editing via Vision In-Context Relations":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new image editing task called "Edit Transfer."  Instead of relying on text prompts or reference images for style transfer, the method learns the underlying transformation between a source image and its edited version and applies that transformation to a new, query image. The method uses a small dataset of carefully constructed image pairs and fine-tunes a DiT-based text-to-image model with LoRA to achieve this. The key idea is to use a visual "in-context" learning approach, drawing inspiration from how Large Language Models (LLMs) can adapt to new tasks from a few examples.  The authors demonstrate the ability to transfer complex spatial edits (e.g., pose changes) and even combine multiple edits, outperforming existing text-based and reference-based editing methods with minimal training data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to image editing that addresses limitations of existing text-based and reference-based methods. The idea of "Edit Transfer" itself is a worthwhile contribution, shifting the paradigm from manipulating latent spaces with textual prompts or directly transferring visual features to learning explicit edit transformations. Using visual in-context learning is also a novel adaptation of techniques from the LLM domain. While the use of DiT and LoRA is not groundbreaking in itself, the way they're combined to achieve this novel task is a key contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper has potential significance because it offers a more flexible and potentially controllable approach to image editing. By learning transformations directly from image pairs, it can capture complex spatial relationships that are difficult to express with text alone. The fact that it can be done with a very small dataset opens possibilities for user-specific or personalized editing workflows. Demonstrating compositional editing capabilities is also significant, as it points towards a more versatile editing system.  The results convincingly demonstrate the method's capabilities on challenging non-rigid edits where other methods falter.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the Edit Transfer problem and explains its motivation.
    *   **Effective Approach:** The proposed method combines existing techniques (DiT, LoRA) in a novel way to achieve the desired outcome.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The qualitative and quantitative results show a clear advantage over existing methods, especially on tasks involving complex spatial transformations. The user study further strengthens the claims.
    *   **Minimal Data Requirement:** Achieving good results with a small dataset is a significant advantage, making the method practical for real-world applications.
    *   **Compositionality:** Successfully demonstrating the compositionality of edits is a strong contribution, enhancing the versatility of the approach.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Low-Level Attribute Transfer:** The paper acknowledges limitations in transferring low-level attributes like color. This restricts the applicability of the method to certain types of edits.
    *   **Dependency on Good Examples:** The quality of the learned transformation depends on the quality of the example pairs. Carefully selected examples are critical which might not always be readily available.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The current implementation focuses mainly on human subjects and pose transformations. The generalizability to other types of objects and edits could be further explored.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Analysis:** The paper is primarily an empirical study. A more formal analysis of the learned transformations and their properties could strengthen the contribution.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could influence future research in image editing by shifting focus towards learning explicit transformations and exploiting visual in-context learning. It might inspire new methods for personalized and controllable image manipulation. The work could also have an impact on related fields like animation and virtual reality. The emphasis on low data requirements makes it appealing for applications where large training sets are not feasible.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper builds upon existing technologies, the problem formulation, visual in-context learning approach, and demonstrated ability to perform complex edits with limited data make it a significant contribution. The limitations, although acknowledged, prevent a higher score. Therefore,

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LEAVS: An LLM-based Labeler for Abdominal CT Supervision
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13330v1)
- **Authors**: Ricardo Bigolin Lanfredi, Yan Zhuang, Mark Finkelstein, Praveen Thoppey Srinivasan Balamuralikrishna, Luke Krembs, Brandon Khoury, Arthi Reddy, Pritam Mukherjee, Neil M. Rofsky, Ronald M. Summers
- **Abstract**: Extracting structured labels from radiology reports has been employed to create vision models to simultaneously detect several types of abnormalities. However, existing works focus mainly on the chest region. Few works have been investigated on abdominal radiology reports due to more complex anatomy and a wider range of pathologies in the abdomen. We propose LEAVS (Large language model Extractor for Abdominal Vision Supervision). This labeler can annotate the certainty of presence and the urgency of seven types of abnormalities for nine abdominal organs on CT radiology reports. To ensure broad coverage, we chose abnormalities that encompass most of the finding types from CT reports. Our approach employs a specialized chain-of-thought prompting strategy for a locally-run LLM using sentence extraction and multiple-choice questions in a tree-based decision system. We demonstrate that the LLM can extract several abnormality types across abdominal organs with an average F1 score of 0.89, significantly outperforming competing labelers and humans. Additionally, we show that extraction of urgency labels achieved performance comparable to human annotations. Finally, we demonstrate that the abnormality labels contain valuable information for training a single vision model that classifies several organs as normal or abnormal. We release our code and structured annotations for a public CT dataset containing over 1,000 CT volumes.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LEAVS paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LEAVS (Large language model Extractor for Abdominal Vision Supervision), a novel framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to automatically extract structured labels from abdominal CT radiology reports. LEAVS aims to address the gap in existing research, which primarily focuses on chest radiology reports, by handling the more complex anatomy and broader range of pathologies found in the abdomen. The framework employs a specialized chain-of-thought prompting strategy with a locally-run LLM, using sentence filtration and multiple-choice questions within a tree-based decision system. The extracted labels include certainty of presence and urgency levels for various abnormalities across nine abdominal organs. The paper demonstrates that LEAVS outperforms competing labelers and even humans in abnormality labeling, achieving comparable performance in urgency assessment. Finally, it shows that these automatically extracted labels can be used to train a vision model for classifying organs as normal or abnormal. The authors release their code and annotations for a public CT dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Strength:** The primary novelty lies in adapting LLM-based label extraction specifically for abdominal CT reports. While LLMs have been used for radiology report processing before, the focus on abdominal CT, with its greater complexity, is a valuable contribution. The specialized chain-of-thought prompting strategy tailored to this domain further strengthens the novelty. Sentence filtering, finding type definitions specific for abdominal CT and urgency assessments are also contributions.
    *   **Weakness:** The individual components (LLM prompting, label extraction, vision model training) are not entirely novel in themselves. The novelty lies in the combination and application to a specific domain. The dependence on a locally-run LLM, while offering privacy and control, potentially limits accessibility for researchers without sufficient computational resources.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Strength:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to accelerate the development of vision models for abdominal imaging. The creation of a large, structured, and publicly available dataset of annotated abdominal CT scans is a significant resource for the research community. The demonstration that the extracted labels can be effectively used to train a vision model is a strong validation of the approach. The improvement over human-level performance is also a significant achievement.
    *   **Weakness:** The vision model training is presented as a proof-of-concept. The results presented show that the findings provide a valuable way of training a vision model to classify organs as normal or abnormal, but more detailed experiments are needed to prove that these findings are also useful in localizing focal findings. The relatively small size of the initial validation dataset (200 reports) could limit the generalizability of the findings. The high inference time also limits the practical use in real time.

*   **Rigor:**

    *   **Strength:** The paper employs rigorous evaluation methodologies, including comparisons against multiple baselines (MAPLEZ, SARLE, humans) and statistical significance testing.
    *   **Weakness:** It would strengthen the paper to describe how was the bias in the reports evaluated and how did the authors try to overcome it.

*   **Overall:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field, offering a novel and effective approach for extracting structured labels from abdominal CT reports. The released dataset and code have the potential to catalyze further research in this area. While the individual components may not be entirely new, the combination and adaptation to the specific domain of abdominal CT is a valuable contribution. The limitation in the ability to test models for visualizing focal points, limitations in datasets, and high running time bring the score down from perfect.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LearnMate: Enhancing Online Education with LLM-Powered Personalized Learning Plans and Support
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13340v1)
- **Authors**: Xinyu Jessica Wang, Christine Lee, Bilge Mutlu
- **Abstract**: With the increasing prevalence of online learning, adapting education to diverse learner needs remains a persistent challenge. Recent advancements in artificial intelligence (AI), particularly large language models (LLMs), promise powerful tools and capabilities to enhance personalized learning in online educational environments. In this work, we explore how LLMs can improve personalized learning experiences by catering to individual user needs toward enhancing the overall quality of online education. We designed personalization guidelines based on the growing literature on personalized learning to ground LLMs in generating tailored learning plans. To operationalize these guidelines, we implemented LearnMate, an LLM-based system that generates personalized learning plans and provides users with real-time learning support. We discuss the implications and future directions of this work, aiming to move beyond the traditional one-size-fits-all approach by integrating LLM-based personalized support into online learning environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LearnMate: Enhancing Online Education with LLM-Powered Personalized Learning Plans and Support":

**Summary:**

The paper presents LearnMate, an LLM-based system designed to enhance personalized learning experiences in online education. The system generates tailored learning plans and offers real-time support to users. The authors derive personalization guidelines based on existing literature, focusing on dimensions like goals, time, pace, and learning paths. LearnMate utilizes LLMs to create customized learning schedules and provide contextual assistance to students using platforms like Khan Academy (via YouTube's API). The paper compares LearnMate's performance with a single-agent LLM, highlighting the advantages of a structured approach for generating adaptive and context-aware support.  The work positions itself as a move towards overcoming the "one-size-fits-all" approach prevalent in traditional online learning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty and Significance:*

The novelty of the paper lies in its systematic approach to integrating LLMs with established personalization guidelines for online learning environments. While LLMs have been applied in education before, LearnMate distinguishes itself by:

1.  *Structured Personalization Framework:*  Instead of relying solely on the inherent capabilities of the LLM, the authors introduce a clear framework grounded in established pedagogical principles for personalization.  This framework provides explicit guidelines for what dimensions of personalization matter and how they should be incorporated into LLM prompts and system design. This makes the LLM a guided tool rather than a standalone "black box."
2.  *Real-time, Contextual Support via YouTube API:* The use of the YouTube API to access Khan Academy content, combined with real-time transcript processing and learning history tracking, is a practical approach to providing relevant and timely assistance.  This demonstrates a pragmatic solution for delivering personalized support using readily available resources.
3.  *Comparative Evaluation Against a Single-Agent LLM:* The direct comparison between LearnMate and a monolithic LLM approach is valuable. It helps to quantify the benefits of the structured personalization framework and the multi-agent system design.  This highlights the importance of a well-designed architecture compared to simply relying on the raw power of a large language model.

*Strengths:*

*   *Clear Problem Definition:* The paper clearly articulates the challenges of personalized learning in online education and motivates the need for better solutions.
*   *Well-Defined Framework:* The four-dimensional personalization guidelines offer a structured approach to tailoring learning plans.
*   *Practical Implementation:* The description of LearnMate's architecture and functionality is comprehensive, detailing how LLMs are used to create personalized learning plans and provide real-time support.
